# CompactBench — handover review, August 2026

> Full engineering review at head `8bb1f62`. 10 parallel reviewers with an adversarial verification pass;
> the six claims in *Reproduced first-hand* were re-run by hand. Findings not marked as reproduced are
> reviewer-reported and should be confirmed before acting on the larger ones.

**45 P0 · 102 P1 · 51 P2** — 127 days since last commit, 0 leaderboard rows, 479 tests passing.

## The verdict

CompactBench is not a neglected codebase. It is a healthy codebase with a broken front door and a scoring
engine that does not yet measure what the project claims. Main is clean — 479 tests pass, ruff and pyright
are green, the contracts are typed and frozen.

But the release on PyPI predates fifteen merged PRs and ships **zero benchmark content**: installed from
PyPI into a clean venv, the README's headline command exits 1. That is the mechanical answer to issue #38.
The submission workflow can never succeed — it resolves the submission directory with a `git diff` against
a commit absent from its own shallow checkout. And the scorer has structural defects that would make a
first leaderboard indefensible.

**Recommendation: do not publish a single number on the current scorer.** Fix the front door this week.
Fix the scorer before publishing results.

## Reproduced first-hand

| # | Finding | Evidence |
|---|---------|----------|
| 1 | `pip install compactbench` cannot run a benchmark | Wheel has 66 entries, zero benchmark content; `compactbench suites list` → `no benchmarks directory`, exit 1. `force-include` landed in PR #21, after the v0.1.0 tag. |
| 2 | Submission pipeline can never succeed | `fetch-depth: 1` + `git diff <base> <head>` → `fatal: bad revision`, swallowed by `\|\| true`, exits with a misleading "PR does not touch any submissions/" error. |
| 3 | Benchmark leaks its own answer key | Turns carry `tags`, including a literal `critical_constraint`. A 5-line non-LLM filter: 36 turns → 4, 9× compression, 2/2 answers retained. |
| 4 | Drift resistance rewards total failure | `clamp(1 + mean(delta))` scores 1.0 for a method failing every cycle. Observed: every case 1.000 while case scores were 0.2–0.6. |
| 5 | "Elite" transcripts are 531 tokens | Measured with the repo's tokenizer: easy 134.6 / medium 187.4 / hard 303.1 / elite 531.1 mean. The regime claimed begins ~100k. |
| 6 | Docs describe a different project | README says 3 families / 15 templates; CLI reports 4 / 20. Cost catalogue names retired models. `[Unreleased]` empty across 15 PRs. |

## The order of work

### Phase 1 — earn the right to be looked at (week 1–2)

- Cut **0.2.0** from current main; add a release gate that installs the wheel into a clean venv and asserts `compactbench suites list` exits 0.
- Fix the evaluate workflow's shallow checkout; create the `evaluate` label (it does not exist in the repo).
- Answer **issue #38** with a dated commitment.
- Merge **PR #37** (OpenAI `base_url`) — only outside contribution, 19 days waiting, unlocks vLLM/llama.cpp/LM Studio/OpenRouter. Note it has never had CI run: fork workflows need first-time-contributor approval.
- Clear the 6 dependabot PRs; re-sync `uv.lock` (untouched since the scaffold commit).
- Update the README to the project that exists: four families, six providers, current models.

### Phase 2 — earn the right to be believed (week 3–6)

- Strip `Turn.tags` before the compactor sees the transcript.
- Add **oracle** (full-context) and **null** (empty-artifact) controls — without them no score is interpretable.
- Redefine drift resistance against the oracle, not against cycle zero.
- Replace substring grading with a judge where paraphrase is legitimate.
- Scale transcripts to 50–200k tokens; build an `agent_trace` suite from real trajectories.
- Recalibrate the compression floors (Elite-Mid currently demands a ≤41-token artifact — every honest submission is auto-disqualified).
- Bind provenance: re-derive aggregates from the event log in CI; refuse hand-edited results.
- **Then** publish baselines — ~$37 for the full 4×4 matrix by the repo's own projector.

### Phase 3 — earn citations (this quarter)

- Reposition to "independent, reproducible scores for the compaction your agent already runs."
- Run the multi-cycle head-to-head of provider-native vs framework compaction vs oracle.
- Ship an Inspect AI adapter (five strategies shipped with no quality evaluation attached).
- Write a short systems report; `docs/faq.md` still says "we don't have a paper yet."
- Fix the bus factor — one collaborator owns every CODEOWNERS path.

**Do not do this quarter:** new template families, the custom domain, the Elite governance apparatus
(anti-gaming machinery for a board with zero entries), or the "hardest benchmark program we maintain" claim.

## What changed outside

| Work | Date | Result | Bearing |
|------|------|--------|---------|
| [Governance Decay / ConstraintRot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22528) | Jun 2026 | Violations 0% full-context → 30% post-compaction, peak 59%, 1,323 episodes | The result CompactBench was built to produce |
| [Lost in Compaction / COMPINT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11242) | Jul 2026 | Compactors retain 17% of session constraints; extractor reaches 90%+ | Closest comparable; **no leaderboard** |
| [Letta Context-Bench](https://www.letta.com/blog/context-bench/) | live | Agentic context *engineering* leaderboard | Adjacent; proves a standing board draws attention |
| [Slipstream](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.08580) | 2026 | Trajectory-grounded compaction validation | Contested ground |

**The read:** the finding is taken, the scoreboard is not. Neither paper maintains a standing, vendor-neutral,
reproducible ranking of the compactors people actually ship. That, plus multi-cycle drift, is the defensible
position — but only from a scorer that survives review.

## Full register

All 198 findings by area, sorted by severity. Effort: S = hours, M = days, L = weeks.

### Release & staleness

*What has rotted in four months — 15 findings*

The repo's code is in far better shape than its shipped artifacts. Main is healthy (479 tests pass, ruff/pyright clean), but everything a new user actually touches is 4 months stale or outright broken: the PyPI wheel published on 2026-04-17 was built from the v0.1.0 tag (PR #12) and is missing all 15 subsequent PRs — it ships **zero benchmark content**, so `pip install compactbench && compactbench suites list` fails with "no benchmarks directory", and it has no `anthropic`/`openai` providers that the docs tell users to use. That single broken artifact plausibly explains issue #38 verbatim ("not a single run result — what to compare with?"). Beneath that: `uv.lock` is out of sync with `pyproject.toml` and CI never passes `--locked`, so a reproducibility-branded benchmark re-resolves dependencies on every run; the `--estimate` cost catalogue is a 4-month-old snapshot of models that are now retired; and both hosted providers send `temperature`/`max_tokens` parameters that current Claude and OpenAI reasoning models reject with a 400. Cutting a 0.2.0 release is the highest-leverage action available and unblocks almost everything else.

- **[P0 · STALE · S]** The published PyPI wheel ships no benchmark content — `pip install compactbench` cannot run a single benchmark
  - *Evidence:* PyPI has only compactbench 0.1.0, uploaded 2026-04-17T22:29. The v0.1.0 tag (remote ref e5257997) points at PR #12 `ci: switch hidden-repo auth...`, so the wheel predates PR #21 `fix(post-launch): wheel bundling`. I downloaded the live wheel and inspected it: 66 entries, **0** matching `_data` or `benchmark` — the `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]` mapping at pyproject.toml:88-90 that bundles `benchmarks/public` was added after the tag. Installed into a clean 3.12 venv and ran from an empty directory: `compactbench suites list` → `no benchmarks directory at benchmarks/public`. `compactbench providers list` → only google-ai-studio, groq, mock, ollama. The wheel is also missing providers/anthropic.py, providers/openai.py, providers/counting.py, runner/costs.py, runner/estimate.py, scoring/diagnostics.py, and the whole integrations/ package (61 modules published vs 69 in src/ today).
  - *Why:* README.md:29 makes `pip install compactbench` the headline "30-second try", and docs/getting-started.md:11 repeats it as the only install path. Every user who follows the front door gets a package that cannot execute the benchmark at all. Issue #38 ("not a single run result - why is that? What to compare with?") is exactly the symptom an outside user would report. No amount of leaderboard seeding fixes adoption while the installable artifact is inert.
  - *Fix:* Cut 0.2.0 from current main today: bump `version` in pyproject.toml, move CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` into a `[0.2.0]` section, `git tag -s v0.2.0 && git push origin v0.2.0`, approve the `pypi` environment. Then add a release-gate job to .github/workflows/release.yml that installs the built wheel into a clean venv in an empty directory and asserts `compactbench suites list` exits 0 and lists `elite_practice` — a two-line smoke that would have caught this in April. Consider yanking 0.1.0 on PyPI once 0.2.0 is up.
- **[P0 · STALE · S]** 15 merged PRs are unreleased while CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` is empty, understating what actually shipped
  - *Evidence:* CHANGELOG.md:8 is a bare `## [Unreleased]` with no entries, yet `git log v0.1.0..HEAD` shows 15 commits (PRs #13-#28) including Anthropic+OpenAI providers (#19), prompt caching (#23), case-level parallelism + `--estimate` (#22), LangChain (#16) and LlamaIndex (#17) adapters, the Colab notebook (#18), the reference_resolution template family (#27), and TokenUsage telemetry (#28). Independent confirmation of the drift: the live PyPI metadata carries `Development Status :: 2 - Pre-Alpha`, while pyproject.toml:22 now says `3 - Alpha` — a classifier change sitting unreleased. README.md:95 still describes providers as "(Groq / Google AI Studio / Ollama)", omitting the Anthropic and OpenAI providers that landed in #19.
  - *Why:* A prospective submitter evaluating the project reads the CHANGELOG to judge whether it is alive. An empty `[Unreleased]` under a 4-month-old release reads as abandoned, when in fact a substantial amount of good work is sitting in main. It also means the release notes for 0.2.0 have to be reconstructed from git archaeology rather than maintained incrementally.
  - *Fix:* Populate CHANGELOG.md `[Unreleased]` from `git log v0.1.0..HEAD` now, then rename it to `## [0.2.0] — 2026-08-23` as part of the release in the previous finding. Update README.md:95 to list all six providers. Add a CI check (or a PR-template checkbox) requiring a CHANGELOG entry on any PR touching `src/`, so `[Unreleased]` can never silently drift empty again.
- **[P1 · RISK · S]** uv.lock is out of sync with pyproject.toml and CI never enforces the lock — dependency resolution is unpinned on every run
  - *Evidence:* `git log -1 -- uv.lock` shows the lock has not been touched since the initial scaffold commit (2026-04-16), before the `providers` extra gained anthropic and openai in PR #19. The committed lock contains 90 packages and has **no `anthropic` and no `openai` entries at all**; a full resolve produces 145. I restored the committed lock and ran `uv lock --check`: `The lockfile at 'uv.lock' needs to be updated, but '--locked' was provided.` Meanwhile every workflow calls bare `uv sync` with no `--locked`/`--frozen` (.github/workflows/ci.yml:27,43,61,85; docs.yml:28; evaluate-submission.yml:46; update-leaderboard.yml:38), so uv silently re-resolves against live PyPI on every CI run and every leaderboard evaluation.
  - *Why:* The README's central claim is "Everything is deterministic, versioned, and reproducible." Ranked submissions are scored by evaluate-submission.yml against whatever dependency set PyPI happens to serve that minute. Two identical submissions evaluated a week apart can run different tiktoken (which drives the compression-ratio metric) and different provider SDKs. This directly undermines the trustworthiness of any number the leaderboard eventually publishes — and it is much cheaper to fix now, with zero entries, than after numbers are public.
  - *Fix:* Run `uv lock` and commit the regenerated lock (this also resolves most of the dependabot backlog in one commit). Then change all seven `uv sync` invocations to `uv sync --locked` (`--all-extras --dev --locked` in ci.yml) so CI fails loudly on lock drift instead of papering over it. Pin the `tiktoken` floor tighter than `>=0.7` since it determines scoring output.
- **[P1 · STALE · M]** `--estimate` prints dollar figures from a 4-month-old catalogue of largely retired models, with the staleness marker never surfaced
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/runner/costs.py:16 sets `CATALOGUE_UPDATED = "2026-04-18"`. Grepping the whole repo, that constant is **read nowhere** — it is not exported, not printed by `format_estimate()` (src/compactbench/runner/estimate.py:130-170), and not asserted by any test. The table itself (costs.py:31-48) prices models that are retired or superseded: `mixtral-8x7b-32768` (costs.py:33, decommissioned by Groq), `claude-3-5-haiku-latest`/`claude-3-5-sonnet-latest`/`claude-opus-4-latest` (costs.py:35-37), `gpt-4o`/`gpt-4o-mini`/`o1-mini` (costs.py:39-41), `gemini-2.0-flash-exp`/`gemini-1.5-pro` (costs.py:43,45). The current Claude lineup (claude-opus-5 at $5/$25, claude-sonnet-5 at $3/$15, claude-haiku-4-5 at $1/$5) has no entry, so `lookup_cost` returns None and estimate.py:137-140 tells the user to go edit costs.py.
  - *Why:* `--estimate` exists to answer "can I afford to run this?" — the first question a new user asks and the one that gates whether they run the benchmark at all. Today it either prints a confidently wrong number from retired pricing or refuses to answer for every current model. There is no visible cue that the figure is a snapshot, so users trust it.
  - *Fix:* Refresh costs.py:31-48 with current models (add claude-opus-5, claude-sonnet-5, claude-haiku-4-5, current Groq/Gemini/OpenAI SKUs; drop mixtral-8x7b-32768) and bump CATALOGUE_UPDATED. Make the marker load-bearing: append a line to `format_estimate()` reading `prices as of {CATALOGUE_UPDATED} — verify against the provider` and add a unit test that fails when `CATALOGUE_UPDATED` is more than 90 days old, so the catalogue can never rot silently again.
- **[P1 · BUG · M]** Anthropic provider unconditionally sends `temperature`, which current Claude models reject with a 400
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/providers/base.py:39 defaults `temperature: float = 0.0` on every CompletionRequest, and src/compactbench/providers/anthropic.py:89 puts it into `messages.create` kwargs unconditionally (there is no capability check anywhere in the file). Sampling parameters (`temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k`) were removed on Claude Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, Opus 5, Sonnet 5 and Fable 5 — sending `temperature` returns a 400 invalid_request_error. The module docstring at anthropic.py:1 still reads "Claude 3.5 Sonnet, 3.5 Haiku, Opus 4, etc.", naming only models from the generation this code was written against.
  - *Why:* docs/getting-started.md:37 recommends Anthropic as one of the two providers for real evaluation runs (Groq's free tier can't carry a full Elite suite). Anyone pointing this at a current Claude model gets an immediate hard failure, not a degraded result. That is a dead end for a user 5 minutes into the project, and it silently narrows the benchmark to models nobody is deploying in production any more.
  - *Fix:* Only include `temperature` when the caller explicitly set it — make `CompletionRequest.temperature` `float | None = None` at base.py:39 and have each provider omit the key when None (determinism is already anchored by seeded case generation, not sampling temperature). Update the anthropic.py:1 docstring to stop naming specific model generations. Add adaptive-thinking passthrough while in there, since compaction quality on current Claude models depends on it.
- **[P1 · BUG · M]** OpenAI provider sends `max_tokens` + `temperature`, both rejected by reasoning models — the exact gap PR #37's author flagged
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/providers/openai.py:76-77 always sends `temperature` and `max_tokens` to `chat.completions.create`. OpenAI deprecated `max_tokens` in favour of `max_completion_tokens`, and the reasoning-model families reject both `max_tokens` and any non-default `temperature`. The pyproject.toml:41 floor is `openai>=1.50` while the resolved SDK is 3.3.1 — two major versions of drift under a floor that permits either. openai.py:1 docstring still reads "GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, o1, etc.". This is the same class of problem jaaabir raised on PR #37 (reasoning models via vLLM need `extra_body`/`chat_template_kwargs` passthrough or eval answers come back as chain-of-thought).
  - *Why:* PR #37 is the project's one high-quality outside contribution, 18 days unreviewed, and its entire purpose is to make the OpenAI provider work against vLLM/llama.cpp/Together/OpenRouter — where reasoning models are the common case. Merging #37 without fixing the parameter surface delivers a base_url that still 400s on the models people most want to test, which wastes the contributor's goodwill.
  - *Fix:* Switch openai.py:77 to `max_completion_tokens`, make `temperature` conditional as in the previous finding, and add an `extra_body` passthrough on CompletionRequest so `chat_template_kwargs` can reach vLLM. Do this as a follow-up commit on top of PR #37 rather than as review churn — merge #37 first (it is well-tested and additive), then layer the parameter fix, and reply to jaaabir on the thread that the reasoning-model gap he flagged is now tracked.
- **[P1 · GAP · M]** The Colab notebook installs the broken PyPI wheel, and the CI smoke test is structurally incapable of catching it
  - *Evidence:* scripts/build_notebook.py:64 emits `!pip install -q 'compactbench[providers]'` (mirrored at notebooks/try_compactbench.ipynb:45) — i.e. the notebook installs from PyPI, which is the content-free 0.1.0 wheel proven broken above. The CI notebook-smoke job (.github/workflows/ci.yml:70-88) runs scripts/smoke_notebook.py, which at line 77 filters extracted commands with `return [c for c in commands if c.startswith("compactbench")]` — dropping the `pip install` line entirely — and then executes against `REPO_ROOT`/`BENCHMARKS_DIR` from the local checkout (smoke_notebook.py:28-30). The notebook also runs `--model llama-3.3-70b-versatile` (build_notebook.py:118,164,243).
  - *Why:* The Colab badge is the first item in README.md:10 and the notebook is pitched as the zero-install front door. It has been broken since April while the CI job that exists specifically to protect it reports green on every PR — the worst possible failure mode, because it actively suppresses the signal. This is the second independent path by which a curious user (like the one who filed #38) hits a wall.
  - *Fix:* Have smoke_notebook.py execute the notebook's install line in an isolated venv rather than filtering it out, and run the subsequent commands against *that* venv with no repo checkout on the path — that makes the job test what Colab actually does. Short term, pin the notebook to `!pip install -q 'compactbench[providers]>=0.2.0'` once 0.2.0 ships.
- **[P1 · STALE · M]** Every user-facing model recommendation names a model from the 2024-2025 generation
  - *Evidence:* Full sweep of hardcoded model strings outside tests: README.md:31 and submissions/_template/method.py:12 (`llama3.2`); docs/getting-started.md:64,74,84,94 (`llama3.2`, `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `claude-3-5-haiku-latest`, `gpt-4o-mini`); docs/integrations.md:31,48,101,116,154,163,198 (`gpt-4o-mini`, `llama-3.3-70b-versatile`); src/compactbench/integrations/langchain.py:243,255 and llamaindex.py:255,264 (same, in docstrings); notebooks/try_compactbench.ipynb:136,211,314; scripts/build_notebook.py:118,164,243; submissions/_template/config.yaml:11 (`llama-3.3-70b-versatile`); docs/architecture/decisions.md:161,164,167 (`llama-3.3-70b-versatile`, `kimi-k2-instruct`, `gemini-2.0-flash`, `llama3.2`); docs/launch/*.md:25,38. `llama3.2` dates to Sept 2024.
  - *Why:* A benchmark that recommends two-year-old models by name signals it is not maintained, and its leaderboard rows get grouped by `target_model` (docs/leaderboard.md) — so the first published numbers would be permanently stamped with obsolete model keys, making them uninteresting to compare against on day one. Since there are zero entries today, this is a free moment to pick the right defaults.
  - *Fix:* Replace with current models in one sweep: `llama3.2` → a current Ollama default; Groq/Gemini/OpenAI keys → current SKUs; `claude-3-5-haiku-latest` → `claude-haiku-4-5` (docs/getting-started.md:84) and add `claude-sonnet-5`/`claude-opus-5` as the quality tier. Then remove the temptation to re-rot: keep the canonical per-provider recommended model in ONE place (extend src/compactbench/runner/costs.py into a small model registry) and have docs reference it, rather than 20 hardcoded copies. Add the registry to the same >90-day freshness test as CATALOGUE_UPDATED.
- **[P1 · IMPROVEMENT · S]** Dependabot triage: 5 of 6 open PRs are safe to batch-merge; the real blocker is the stale lock, not any single bump
  - *Evidence:* Resolved each against the dependency graph via `uv pip tree --invert`: **#31 urllib3** (2.6.3) is transitive via requests ← tiktoken — in the core runtime path but a lockfile-only bump; **#33 idna** (committed lock 3.11 → 3.19) transitive via anyio/httpx; **#35 cryptography** (46.0.7) transitive *only* via google-auth ← google-genai, i.e. reachable solely through the `providers` extra, not the default install; **#32 pymdown-extensions** (10.21.2) transitive via mkdocs-material, docs-only; **#34 actions group** is workflow SHA bumps. Only **#36 pydantic-settings** (2.13.1) is a direct dependency (pyproject.toml:37, floor `>=2.3`) and the only one that can break src/compactbench/config.py — and the full suite already passes locally against 2.13.1.
  - *Why:* Six stale PRs, the oldest from May, are the most visible "is anyone home?" signal on the repo page — more visible than the code quality. Clearing them is cheap and immediately changes how the project reads to the outside contributors arriving now.
  - *Fix:* Do the `uv lock` regeneration from the lock finding FIRST — it likely closes #31/#32/#33/#35 automatically as their bumps land in the regenerated lock. Then merge #34 (actions) and #36 (pydantic-settings) on a green CI run. Afterwards, widen the dependabot grouping at .github/dependabot.yml:19-24 to include `update-types: ["major"]` and security updates in the `python-dependencies` group, so transitive security bumps arrive as one weekly PR instead of six individual ones.
- **[P1 · STALE · S]** Python 3.14 is absent from the test matrix, classifiers, and .python-version
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/ci.yml:50 pins the matrix to `["3.11", "3.12", "3.13"]`; pyproject.toml:25-28 lists classifiers only through 3.13; .python-version is `3.12` and every workflow hardcodes `PYTHON_VERSION: "3.12"` (ci.yml:13, evaluate-submission.yml:23, docs.yml:27, release.yml, update-leaderboard.yml:35). `uv python list` in this environment offers cpython-3.14.0rc2. Python 3.14 has been stable since Oct 2025, so the project's dev default is two releases behind and 3.14 is entirely untested.
  - *Why:* `requires-python = ">=3.11"` is unbounded above, so `pip install compactbench` on a 3.14 interpreter resolves and installs with zero coverage behind it. Users on current Python are the ones most likely to be building agent infrastructure — exactly the audience this benchmark wants — and they will hit any incompatibility first, in an unreleased-for-4-months package.
  - *Fix:* Add "3.14" to the ci.yml:50 matrix and to the pyproject.toml classifier list, and bump `.python-version` + the `PYTHON_VERSION` env in the workflows to 3.13 (keeping 3.11 as the tested floor). Fold this into the 0.2.0 release so the published wheel's metadata advertises current-Python support.
- **[P1 · RISK · S]** pre-commit pins a toolchain roughly two years behind what CI resolves, so local hooks and CI disagree
  - *Evidence:* .pre-commit-config.yaml pins ruff-pre-commit `v0.6.9` (Oct 2024), pyright-python `v1.1.383` (Sept 2024), and pre-commit-hooks `v5.0.0` (Oct 2024). CI resolves ruff 0.15.11 and pyright 1.1.408 via `uv sync --all-extras --dev` against floors `ruff>=0.5` / `pyright>=1.1.370` (pyproject.toml:62-63). Separately, the pyright hook's `additional_dependencies` (.pre-commit-config.yaml:24-27) lists only pydantic, typer, and ruamel.yaml — omitting pydantic-settings, httpx, tiktoken, jsonschema, and rich, all of which src/ imports under `reportMissingImports = true` (pyproject.toml:147) in strict mode.
  - *Why:* A contributor installs pre-commit as CONTRIBUTING.md directs, gets a clean local pass from ruff 0.6.9, then watches CI fail on ruff 0.15.11 formatting — or gets spurious missing-import errors from the under-provisioned pyright hook. For a project actively courting first-time outside contributors (PR #37's author is one), a contribution loop that fails for reasons unrelated to the change is a strong deterrent.
  - *Fix:* Bump the three `rev:` pins to current releases and complete the pyright hook's `additional_dependencies` with pydantic-settings, httpx, tiktoken, jsonschema, and rich. Then close the gap permanently: raise the pyproject floors to `ruff>=0.15` / `pyright>=1.1.400` and add `.pre-commit-config.yaml` to the dependabot `github-actions`/pre-commit ecosystem so the pins stay current automatically.
- **[P2 · BUG · S]** `.env` keys silently do not work for the Anthropic and OpenAI providers, contradicting the docs
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/config.py:26-28 declares only `groq_api_key`, `google_ai_studio_api_key`, and `ollama_base_url` on the pydantic-settings `Settings` class — config.py was never updated when PR #19 added the two providers. anthropic.py:44 and openai.py:40 instead read `os.environ.get(...)` directly, and pydantic-settings does not export `.env` values into `os.environ`. Verified empirically: with a `.env` containing both keys, `Settings().groq_api_key` → `'g-secret'`, `hasattr(s,'anthropic_api_key')` → `False`, `os.environ['COMPACTBENCH_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY']` → `None`, and `AnthropicProvider()` raises `ProviderError: Anthropic API key required`. docs/getting-started.md:53 states "Put keys in `.env` at the project root" for all providers in the table above it.
  - *Why:* The two providers this affects are the same two docs/getting-started.md:37 recommends for real evaluation runs. The failure is confusing rather than obvious — the key is right there in `.env`, and Groq works from the same file — so a user reasonably concludes the tool is broken rather than that this one path is unwired.
  - *Fix:* Add `anthropic_api_key: str | None` and `openai_api_key: str | None` fields to Settings at config.py:26-28, and change anthropic.py:44 / openai.py:40 to fall back to `settings.<provider>_api_key` instead of reading `os.environ` directly. Add one unit test per provider asserting a `.env`-sourced key reaches the constructor, so the two config paths can't diverge again.
- **[P2 · RISK · S]** pyright type-checks against 3.12 while the package supports and CI tests 3.11
  - *Evidence:* pyproject.toml:144 sets `pythonVersion = "3.12"` while pyproject.toml:8 declares `requires-python = ">=3.11"` and ci.yml:50 runs tests on 3.11. ruff is configured consistently with the floor (`target-version = "py311"`, pyproject.toml:129), so pyright is the odd one out.
  - *Why:* Type constructs valid in 3.12 but not 3.11 (PEP 695 generics, the newer type-parameter syntax) pass pyright and only surface as a runtime SyntaxError on the 3.11 matrix leg — or, worse, for a 3.11 user of the published wheel, since the typecheck job is the one gate meant to catch exactly this class of issue.
  - *Fix:* Set `pythonVersion = "3.11"` at pyproject.toml:144 to match the declared floor, so pyright validates against the oldest supported interpreter. If 3.11 support is not actually wanted, the cleaner alternative is to raise `requires-python` to `>=3.12` and drop 3.11 from the ci.yml:50 matrix — but pick one, don't leave them disagreeing.
- **[P2 · STALE · S]** Dependency floors are so old they no longer constrain anything, spanning major versions with breaking changes
  - *Evidence:* pyproject.toml floors vs what actually resolves: `anthropic>=0.40` → 1.0.0 (a 0.x→1.x major with real breaking changes: httpx2 backend, awaited async `.with_raw_response`, removed deprecated params); `openai>=1.50` → 3.3.1 (two majors); `ruff>=0.5` → 0.15.11; `typer>=0.12` → 0.24.1; `rich>=13.7` → 15.0.0; `pytest>=8.2` → 9.0.3; `tiktoken>=0.7` → 0.12.0.
  - *Why:* `anthropic>=0.40` permits both a 0.x SDK (where this provider code would need different call patterns) and 1.x. With no committed lock entry for anthropic at all (see the lock finding), a fresh install can land anywhere in that range — including combinations nobody has ever run. For a benchmark whose numbers are supposed to be reproducible, "any version from the last two years" is not a specification.
  - *Fix:* Raise floors to what is actually tested and cap the SDKs that have shipped breaking majors: `anthropic>=1,<2`, `openai>=3,<4`, `tiktoken>=0.12` (it drives the compression metric), `ruff>=0.15`, `pytest>=9`. Do this in the same commit as the `uv lock` regeneration so pyproject and the lock become consistent in one move.
- **[P2 · STALE · S]** README claims 15 hidden ranked templates that the CHANGELOG says never landed, and no submission can be evaluated without them
  - *Evidence:* README.md:99 states the v0.1.0 content includes "15 public Elite practice templates + **15 hidden ranked templates** across three launch families". CHANGELOG.md:52, describing the same release, says the opposite: "Hidden ranked set (20 per family in `compactbench/compactbench-hidden`) **lands in a follow-up** so it doesn't commingle public and hidden content" — and no follow-up appears in `git log v0.1.0..HEAD`. evaluate-submission.yml:97-101 hard-fails when `HIDDEN_REPO_TOKEN` is absent (deliberately, per the comment at :88-95: "silently falling back to public elite_practice used to produce a 'ranked-looking' result... Fix the secret or don't rank"). I could not verify compactbench-hidden's existence — this session's GitHub access is scoped to compactbench/compactbench only.
  - *Why:* Three numbers disagree across two files (15 vs 20-per-family vs never-landed), which is corrosive on its own for a benchmark selling versioned rigour. More practically: if the hidden repo or the secret is missing, the ranked path cannot run at all, which combined with the broken wheel fully explains zero submissions and gives a concrete answer to issue #38.
  - *Fix:* First confirm whether `compactbench/compactbench-hidden` exists and `HIDDEN_REPO_TOKEN` is configured — that determines whether the submission pipeline is live or notional. Then reconcile README.md:99 and CHANGELOG.md:52 to state the true count and status. Whichever way it resolves, reply to issue #38 with the honest answer (the wheel is broken, 0.2.0 is coming, here is the ranked-set status) — an answered issue from an interested outsider is worth more right now than any code change.

### Scoring & validity

*Whether the numbers mean anything — 20 findings*

The scoring engine is clean, well-typed, well-tested code that measures the wrong thing. Three of the four headline metrics are structurally broken: an artifact containing literally nothing scores elite_score 0.608 (measured with the repo's own scoring code), drift_resistance is 1.000 for every case because the compactor is re-handed the full original transcript at every cycle, and contradiction_rate is hard-wired to 0.00 for an entire template family. The graders are exact-substring matchers that reject 6 of 8 realistic correct LLM phrasings while crediting content-free answers, and the leaderboard publishes submitter-supplied JSON that nothing ever recomputes — I forged a results.jsonl to elite_score 0.994 whose own per-item data recomputes to 0.36, and qualify() passed it with zero objections. There is no validity evidence of any kind (no human agreement, no downstream correlation, no variance, no error bars, no n) and no audit trail: model responses and artifacts are discarded, so no published number can ever be re-scored or checked. Publishing a first leaderboard on this engine would be worse than publishing nothing.

- **[P0 · BUG · M]** An artifact containing nothing scores elite_score 0.608 — ignorance is rewarded
  - *Evidence:* `forbidden_absent` (src/compactbench/scoring/checks.py:30-34) returns 1.0 whenever the string is absent, which includes empty strings, refusals and "I don't know". `score_item` is fed `responses.get(item.key, "")` (src/compactbench/scoring/scorer.py:78), so even a missing response scores a free 1.0. Across benchmarks/public/elite_practice, 19 of 52 eval items are `forbidden_absent` = 35.9% of total item weight, and 9 of the 15 `forbidden_behavior_retention` items (the ones that constitute constraint_retention, src/compactbench/runner/persistence.py:250-256) use it. I ran the repo's real scoring code over all 20 elite-practice templates with `CompactionArtifact()` (every section empty) and the response "The artifact does not contain that information." for every item: overall_score 0.333, constraint_retention 0.375, contradiction_rate 0.000, compression_ratio 451x (src/compactbench/scoring/compression.py:66 floors the denominator at 1, so an empty artifact yields transcript_tokens/1), drift_resistance 1.000 → elite_score 0.608 at every tier. `compactbench score` on a real mock-provider run reproduces the identical aggregates (overall 0.375, constraint 0.400, ratio 105x).
  - *Why:* This is the number a first submitter would beat by doing nothing. Issue #38 asks "what to compare with?" — if the answer is a leaderboard where a no-op scores 0.61 and constraint_retention 0.375, the benchmark's central claim (that it measures whether constraints survive compaction) is falsified on day one by anyone who reads checks.py.
  - *Fix:* Make `forbidden_absent` a conjunction, not a negation. Add a required companion field so the spec becomes `{check: rejects_forbidden, forbidden: "<X>", must_show: "<the correct alternative>"}` scoring 1.0 only when the forbidden item is absent AND the correct alternative is present — i.e. the response proves knowledge rather than proving silence. Update the 23 `forbidden_absent` items in benchmarks/public/**. Separately, make a missing key in `responses` raise instead of defaulting to "" (scorer.py:78). Add tests/unit/test_scoring_null_compactor.py asserting an empty artifact scores < 0.15 elite_score on every public suite, and wire it into CI as a permanent floor test.
- **[P0 · RISK · M]** Leaderboard publishes submitter-supplied numbers that nothing recomputes — forgery demonstrated
  - *Evidence:* `to_run_result` (src/compactbench/runner/persistence.py:174-182) takes overall_score, drift_resistance, constraint_retention, contradiction_rate and compression_ratio verbatim from the `run_end` line and never compares them to `aggregate_run_metrics(cases)` — the recompute path at persistence.py:185-193 runs only when run_end is *missing*. scripts/rebuild_leaderboard.py:60-63 calls `to_run_result` on whatever `submissions/<handle>/<method>/results.jsonl` is in the merged tree and projects it straight to the public leaderboard. .github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml:170-210 commits the canonical hidden-set results back to the PR branch **only** when `github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository` — i.e. never for a fork PR, which is how every outside contributor submits (PR #37 is a fork PR); the fork path is a `core.warning` asking a human to remember. Proof: I took a real mock run, edited only the numeric fields in the run_end line and case_score in the case lines, and got `qualify(... tier="Elite-Aggressive") -> qualified=True, reasons=[]` and published elite_score 0.994, while the per-ItemScore records inside that same file recompute to a mean item score of 0.3636 over 66 items and cycle scores of {0.2, 0.5, 0.6}. Nothing flagged the inconsistency. `published_at` — a tie-breaker in ranking.py:66-67 — is also taken from the submitter-controlled `completed_at`.
  - *Why:* The entire value of a leaderboard is that the numbers are the maintainer's, not the submitter's. Right now the trust boundary depends on a maintainer manually downloading a workflow artifact for every fork PR. The first forged or merely stale entry that gets caught destroys the project's credibility permanently, and with zero entries today there is no reputational buffer.
  - *Fix:* (1) In `to_run_result`, always recompute aggregates from the case events and raise if the stored run_end values differ by more than 1e-6 — treat divergence as tampering, not rounding. (2) In rebuild_leaderboard.py, reject any run whose `suite_key` is not the hidden ranked suite and whose `scorer_version` is not the current SCORER_VERSION. (3) Have evaluate-submission.yml emit an HMAC over (run_id, suite_version, scorer_version, aggregates) using a repo secret, store it in the run_end `notes`, and have rebuild_leaderboard.py refuse rows without a valid signature — that closes the fork hole without depending on a human. (4) Set `published_at` from the merge commit timestamp, not from submitter data.
- **[P0 · BUG · M]** drift_resistance measures nothing: the compactor gets the full original transcript back every cycle
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/runner/cycle.py:68-79 sets `working_transcript = transcript` and appends one continuation pair, then calls `compactor.compact(working_transcript, previous_artifact=...)`. src/compactbench/runner/run.py:255 feeds `result.extended_transcript` — the *whole* accumulated transcript — into the next cycle. So at cycle 2 the compactor still sees every original turn, including the buried constraint. docs/methodology.md:127 states the opposite: "your compactor is called *again* with only the previous artifact as context. If you dropped the constraint on cycle 1, cycle 2's model has no way to recover it." That is not what the code does. Empirically, every case in a 3-cycle run reports drift_resistance 1.000 (verified via `compactbench score` on an 8-case starter run: all eight cases 1.000).
  - *Why:* Multi-cycle drift is advertised as the benchmark's first-class differentiator and is 30% of elite_score (ranking.py:43). As implemented it is a near-constant 1.0 for any stateless compactor, so 30% of the ranking signal is dead weight and the remaining variation is provider sampling noise. Worse, the docs describe behaviour the code does not implement, so the first careful reviewer finds a documented-vs-actual contradiction in the flagship metric.
  - *Fix:* Change cycle.py so that for cycle_number >= 1 the transcript handed to `compact()` is reconstructed as (previous artifact rendered as a system/context turn) + the continuation turns only — never the original turns. Keep the full transcript separately as the compression baseline so `source_transcript` still measures a true ratio. Add a test asserting that for cycle >= 1 no original turn id appears in the transcript passed to the compactor. If you deliberately want the current "full history re-compaction" semantics instead, rename the metric (it is re-compaction stability, not drift) and fix methodology.md:127 — but then it should not be 30% of the score.
- **[P0 · BUG · L]** Substring graders reject correct answers: 6 of 8 realistic LLM phrasings score 0
  - *Evidence:* `_normalize` (src/compactbench/scoring/checks.py:18-20) only lowercases and collapses whitespace — no unicode normalization, no punctuation folding, no morphology. Against the actual lexicon strings in src/compactbench/dsl/generators.py:137-162, using the repo's own `contains_normalized`: "Never store passwords in **plaintext**." → 0.0; "Passwords must not be stored in plaintext." → 0.0; "Do not call `eval()` on user input." → 0.0; "Don't blindly trust the X-Forwarded-For header." → 0.0; "No deploying on Friday." → 0.0; "Kestrel & Sons" vs "Kestrel and Sons" → 0.0; curly apostrophe vs ASCII → 0.0; "March 14, 2026" vs "2026-03-14" → 0.0; "$47250" vs "$47,250" → 0.0. The same formatting variation flips `forbidden_absent` the *other* way (bolding the inner word makes a violating answer score 1.0), so surface formatting moves different items in opposite directions. The eval prompt (src/compactbench/runner/evaluation.py:79-82) says only "answer the following question concisely" — nothing asks the model to quote verbatim, and Llama 3.3 70B (the pinned leaderboard model, decisions.md D6) bolds key terms constantly.
  - *Why:* The measured quantity is "did the target model happen to echo the generator's exact byte string", not "did the compaction preserve the state". Two compaction methods can be separated by markdown habits of the target model. Any ML-literate visitor will spot this in checks.py within a minute of clicking through from the leaderboard.
  - *Fix:* Three layers, in this order: (1) immediate, cheap — change build_evaluation_item_suffix (evaluation.py:76-83) to instruct "quote the relevant rule or name verbatim as it was originally written", and add unicode NFKC + punctuation folding (smart quotes, en-dash, &/and, comma-in-number) to `_normalize`; (2) add an `aliases: [...]` list to the expected spec and to the generator lexicons so "deploy on Fridays"/"deploying on Friday" both count; (3) the real fix — add an `llm_judge` check type backed by a pinned cheap judge model with a fixed rubric, run it as a *second* grader, and publish the agreement rate between string-match and judge as a headline reliability number. Ship (1) and (2) before any leaderboard launch.
- **[P0 · RISK · M]** Compression floor uses the run mean, so dumping the raw transcript is rankable
  - *Evidence:* `qualify` compares `run_result.compression_ratio` (qualification.py:41-46) against the tier floor, and that value is the arithmetic mean over every cycle of every case (persistence.py:258-262). `compression_ratio` per cycle is unbounded above because the denominator is floored at 1 (compression.py:66) — a near-empty artifact yields ~450x on an elite case. Meanwhile `CompactionArtifact.structured_state` (contracts/artifact.py:26-32) allows 5 lists x 200 entries x 500 chars plus an entity_map with **no max_length on the dict at all**, i.e. ~125k tokens of storage — far more than any 439-token benchmark transcript, so verbatim raw-dumping is schema-legal. Arithmetic: 20 cases, raw-dump 19 of them (ratio ~1.05, near-perfect recall) and return an empty artifact on 1 (ratio ~450) → mean ratio 21x, which clears the Elite-Aggressive 8x floor with a full 1.0 compression_bonus, while overall_score sits at ~0.91.
  - *Why:* A method that performs zero compaction can take the #1 Elite-Aggressive slot. The compression floor is the only thing standing between the leaderboard and 'paste the transcript', and it is defeated by averaging.
  - *Fix:* Qualify on the per-case *minimum* (or 10th percentile) compression ratio, not the mean, and report both on the leaderboard. Cap the per-cycle ratio at, say, 100x before averaging so one degenerate case cannot carry a run. Add a hard artifact size cap (total tokens <= transcript_tokens / tier_floor, enforced per cycle in score_cycle, with the cycle scored 0 if exceeded) and a `max_length` on `StructuredState.entity_map`. Add a regression test that a raw-dump compactor fails qualification on every tier.
- **[P1 · BUG · S]** contradiction_rate is structurally 0.00 for the entire decision_override family
  - *Evidence:* `contradiction_rate` excludes `forbidden_behavior_retention` and `locked_decision_retention` items from its denominator and returns 0.0 when none remain (src/compactbench/scoring/contradictions.py:67-70). I audited every public template: decision_override_v1_1, _v1_2, _v1_4, _v1_5 and decision_override_starter_v1 contain *only* those two item types, so non_recall is empty and contradiction_rate is 0.000 no matter what the model says — 5 of 24 public templates. The same shape almost certainly recurs in the hidden ranked set, which is built from the same families.
  - *Why:* decision_override is the family whose entire purpose is catching a model that acts on a superseded decision — the canonical contradiction — and the contradiction detector is switched off for it. contradiction_rate is both a published metric and a qualification floor (<= 0.10, qualification.py:48-53), so a submission that contradicts on every decision_override case still passes the floor cleanly.
  - *Fix:* Add an explicit `contradiction_probe` item type (or at minimum one `planning_soundness` item per decision_override template) so every case has at least one non-recall item, and make `validate_template` reject any template with zero non-recall items. Report contradiction_rate as `n/a` rather than `0.000` when the denominator is empty so the inertness is visible instead of flattering.
- **[P1 · BUG · M]** The contradiction detector detects mentions, not contradictions — correct refusals are penalised
  - *Evidence:* `response_violates` (contradictions.py:33-38) returns True if any `ground_truth.forbidden_behaviors` string appears anywhere in the response. It never inspects `ground_truth.locked_decisions`, contradicting docs/methodology.md:57 ("Any response that violates a `locked_decision` or a `forbidden_behavior`"), and it also contradicts decisions.md B3 which defines the denominator as `total_responses` while the code uses non-recall responses only. Because it is pure lexical presence, the ideal answer to a buried_constraint planning item — "Next step: schedule the kickoff. Note we must never store passwords in plaintext." — is counted as a violation, verified: `response_violates` fires and `forbidden_absent` scores 0.0 for that exact string. The model is penalised twice (item score 0 and a contradiction penalty at scorer.py:81) for demonstrating that the constraint survived.
  - *Why:* The penalty term actively selects for compactors whose artifacts make the model *silent* about constraints rather than *aware* of them — the opposite of what a compaction benchmark should reward. And the spec, the docs and the code define the metric three different ways, so no published number can be reproduced from the documentation.
  - *Fix:* Require a negation-scope test: score a violation only when the forbidden string appears without a negating cue in its clause ("never", "must not", "avoid", "do not", "no longer", "instead of"), and prefer the same `llm_judge` path as finding #4 for the real version. Add locked_decision violation detection (a response asserting a superseded decision as current). Then reconcile methodology.md:57-61 and decisions.md B3 with the implementation and bump SCORER_VERSION.
- **[P1 · BUG · S]** drift_resistance math structurally rewards weak methods and returns 1.0 on degenerate input
  - *Evidence:* `drift_resistance` (src/compactbench/scoring/drift.py:19-30) is `clamp(1 + mean(c_n - c_0), 0, 1)` — an *absolute* delta, so the achievable drop is bounded by the baseline. Measured with the repo's function: [0.95, 0.60, 0.55] (strong method losing 40 points) → 0.625, while [0.30, 0.10, 0.05] (weak method, also collapsing) → 0.775. A method scoring 0.0 at every cycle → 1.000. `drift_resistance([])` → 1.000 and `drift_resistance([0.5])` → 1.000 (tests/unit/test_scoring_drift.py:20-22,45-46 lock both in), and `aggregate_run_metrics` returns drift_resistance 1.0 for a run with zero cases (persistence.py:239-246). Averaging also hides catastrophe: [1.0, 0.0, 1.0] and [1.0, 0.5, 0.5] both return 0.500.
  - *Why:* 30% of elite_score is a metric on which failing harder is an advantage and total failure is perfect. Combined with finding #3 (drift is not actually exercised) this term is worse than useless — it inverts the ranking signal in exactly the regime where methods differ.
  - *Fix:* Use a relative retention: `mean(c_n) / c_0` when c_0 > 0, clamped to [0,1], with drift_resistance defined as `None` (excluded from elite_score with the weight redistributed) when c_0 < 0.1 — you cannot measure retention of something that was never retained. Return `None`, not 1.0, for 0 or 1 cycles and for an empty run, and make persistence.py:239-246 propagate that. Report worst-cycle retention alongside mean so a single-cycle collapse is visible.
- **[P1 · GAP · L]** No statistical rigour anywhere — and the CLI claims a std-dev it never computes
  - *Evidence:* A repo-wide grep for confidence/stdev/stderr/variance/bootstrap/interval/significan over src/, docs/ and tests/ returns nothing but a comment in runner/estimate.py:59 and a governance hand-wave in docs/elite-program.md:74. No metric carries an n, a standard error or an interval: LeaderboardRow (leaderboard/projection.py:20-40) has no case count, no seed count and no dispersion field, and docs/leaderboard.md:81 renders bare 3-decimal point estimates. `compactbench score` prints per-case scores and aggregates with no dispersion at all. Meanwhile src/compactbench/cli.py:63-65 tells users "5 is enough for per-family std-dev to be meaningful" — no std-dev is computed anywhere in the codebase. There is also no repeated-seed replication: one run = one seed_group, and provider calls pin temperature 0.0 (providers/base.py:39) but pass no `seed` to Groq/OpenAI, so runs are not reproducible either.
  - *Why:* With ~60 ranked cases whose per-cycle scores are highly correlated (same transcript re-scored each cycle), the effective n is ~60 and the standard error on overall_score is roughly 0.06 — two methods within ~0.12 elite_score are indistinguishable, yet ranking.py:59-67 breaks ties at the 1e-9 level and assigns integer ranks. Publishing ordered ranks without error bars is the single most common reason a benchmark gets dismissed, and it is the first thing a reviewer will ask when entries finally appear.
  - *Fix:* (1) Run every ranked submission over k>=3 seed_groups and report mean +/- 95% bootstrap CI on overall_score, drift_resistance and constraint_retention; add `n_cases`, `n_seed_groups` and `ci_low`/`ci_high` fields to LeaderboardRow and render them in docs/leaderboard.md. (2) Assign *tied ranks* when CIs overlap rather than ordering on noise. (3) Pass a fixed `seed` to providers that accept one. (4) Fix the false std-dev claim in cli.py:63-65 or actually compute and print per-family std-dev in `compactbench score`. (5) Publish a minimum-detectable-difference number in methodology.md so submitters know what a 0.02 gap means.
- **[P1 · GAP · M]** Model responses and artifacts are discarded — no published number can ever be audited or re-scored
  - *Evidence:* `score_item` hard-codes `details={}` (src/compactbench/scoring/scorer.py:42) and `ItemScore` (contracts/result.py:53-61) has no response field. `CycleResult` (contracts/result.py:76-88) stores the scorecard, drift delta, latency and tokens — never the artifact, never the responses. `execute_cycle` (runner/cycle.py:91-97) returns the artifact but run.py:245-253 drops it. So results.jsonl contains scores and nothing that produced them.
  - *Why:* Every fix in this report — a better grader, a judge model, a human-agreement study, a re-score under a bumped SCORER_VERSION — requires the raw responses, and they no longer exist after a run. It also means a disputed leaderboard entry cannot be investigated, and the anti-gaming 'shadow evaluation' promised in docs/elite-program.md:71 has no data to work from. Fixing this is a prerequisite for establishing validity at all, so it should land before the first published run rather than after.
  - *Fix:* Add `response: str` and `expected: dict` to ItemScore (details is already there and unused — populate it with `{response, expected, normalized_response}`), and add `artifact: CompactionArtifact` to CycleResult. Gate raw storage behind a `--record-transcripts` flag defaulting to on for maintainer/hidden runs and off for public convenience runs if size is a concern; a full elite run is ~60 cases x 3 cycles x 3 items, which is trivially small. Then add `compactbench rescore --results r.jsonl` that recomputes every metric from stored responses under the current scorer — that command is also the fix for finding #2's verification gap.
- **[P1 · BUG · M]** Nine templates require the same string to be present in one item and absent in another; five embed the forbidden string in their own prompt
  - *Evidence:* Audit over all 24 public templates: 9 templates have a `contains_normalized` item and a `forbidden_absent` item on the *identical* value (buried_constraint_v1_1..v1_5, entity_confusion_v1_1, reference_resolution_v1_1/_v1_2/_v1_4/_v1_5, plus both starters). Concretely benchmarks/public/starter/buried_constraint_starter_v1.yaml:52-64: `recall_constraint` requires the forbidden action present, `apply_constraint` requires it absent. 5 `forbidden_absent` items embed the forbidden string verbatim in their own prompt, so any echo of the question fails — e.g. entity_confusion_v1_1.yaml:57-62 asks "Does {{person_a}} own {{task_b}}?" and scores 0 unless the answer omits task_b; decision_override_starter_v1 asks "Is 'store passwords in plaintext' still the plan?" and any natural answer ("No, 'store passwords in plaintext' is no longer the plan") scores 0. Verified with the repo's functions: "No - Bob owns the vendor audit; Alice owns the migration." scores 0.0, while the content-free "No." scores 1.0. Eight templates use `forbidden_absent` on a yes/no question this way.
  - *Why:* These items reward terseness and punish exactly the informative, constraint-restating behaviour a good compaction artifact should produce. They are also anti-correlated with the recall items in the same case, so a single response style cannot maximise both — the score partly measures the target model's verbosity, which is a property of the model, not of the compaction method being ranked.
  - *Fix:* Rewrite every yes/no `forbidden_absent` item as an explicit-rejection check: prompt "Does X own Y? Answer with the correct owner." and score with the `rejects_forbidden` check from finding #1 (correct owner present AND no assertion that X owns Y). Never embed the forbidden string in the prompt for a `forbidden_absent` item — add that as a hard rule in `validate_template`, along with a rule rejecting any case where the same value is required-present and required-absent.
- **[P1 · BUG · S]** 7% of entity_confusion cases are degenerate because template variables can collide
  - *Evidence:* `resolve_variables` (src/compactbench/dsl/generators.py:230-237) draws each variable independently from the same lexicon with no uniqueness constraint; person_name has 36 entries, action_phrase 25, project_noun 20. Over 2000 seeds for entity_confusion_v1_1: person_a == person_b in 3.00% of cases, task_a == task_b in 4.00%, 7.00% degenerate overall. Seed 73 produces the transcript turn "On the integration sprint: Kenji owns deploy on Fridays, David owns deploy on Fridays. Clear split." — where `assign_a` and `assign_b` both check for the same string (trivially satisfiable) while `no_swap` requires that string absent (unsatisfiable for a correct answer). The same collision hazard applies to reference_resolution_v1_2 (topic_a/topic_b, early_action/forbidden_action) and every multi-variable template.
  - *Why:* Roughly one ranked case in fourteen is unanswerable-or-trivial noise injected directly into the score, and which cases collide depends on the seed_group — which docs/elite-program.md:70 says rotates on leaderboard version changes. That means the ranking shifts between versions for reasons unrelated to the methods.
  - *Fix:* Add a `distinct: [person_a, person_b]` / `distinct: [task_a, task_b]` declaration to the template DSL and enforce it in `resolve_variables` by resampling with the derived sub-seed until distinct (deterministic, cheap). Add a `validate_template` rule requiring a `distinct` group whenever two variables share a generator, and a generation-time assertion that no two bound values are equal. Regenerate any published case ids after the fix and bump the suite version.
- **[P1 · BUG · S]** reference_resolution_v1_2 asks for both tracks and grades only one
  - *Evidence:* benchmarks/public/elite_practice/reference_resolution_v1_2.yaml:66-71: item `do_not_confuse_tracks`, type `immutable_fact_recall` (weight 2), prompt "Name both tracks the user is juggling." with `check: contains_normalized, value: "{{topic_b}}"`. A model that names only topic_b — losing topic_a, which is precisely the failure the template's own header comment says it is testing — scores a perfect 1.0. `set_match` exists in checks.py:42-48 and is exported, and is used by zero templates across the whole repo.
  - *Why:* The item claimed to catch track-confusion cannot catch it. This is the kind of defect that is invisible until someone reads the YAML, and the hidden ranked set was authored the same way and cannot be reviewed by outsiders — so it needs a mechanical check, not a careful reader.
  - *Fix:* Change to `check: set_match, values: ["{{topic_a}}", "{{topic_b}}"]`. Then add a `validate_template` heuristic that flags any `contains_normalized` item whose prompt contains 'both', 'each', 'all', or 'list' — and run the same audit over the hidden ranked suite before ranking anything.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · L]** No validity evidence of any kind, and no plan to produce it
  - *Evidence:* docs/methodology.md presents the string checks, the weights and elite_score as settled fact with no reliability or validity section; grep across docs/ for human/agreement/inter-rater/validity/correlation returns one unrelated hit in integrations.md. There is no human-labelled subset, no agreement statistic between the automatic grader and a human, no correlation with any downstream agentic task, and no evidence that a higher elite_score means an agent actually behaves better after compaction. The item weights (scoring/scorer.py:22-29) and the elite_score coefficients (leaderboard/ranking.py:44-47) are asserted in decisions.md B3/B4 with no derivation — 0.40/0.30/0.20/0.10 and 3/3/2/2/1/1 appear from nowhere.
  - *Why:* 'Adversarial benchmark for compaction' is a claim about the world; without a validity argument it is a claim about substring matching. Anyone deciding whether to invest a weekend building a submission will ask what the number predicts, and today there is no answer. This is also the cheapest credibility win available: the study is small.
  - *Fix:* Ship a validity appendix before the first leaderboard: (1) hand-label 150 (item, response) pairs from a real run, publish Cohen's kappa between the string grader and human judgement per check type — this also quantifies findings #1 and #4 in a number you can defend; (2) run one downstream task (an agent completing a multi-turn task after compaction, pass/fail) on 3-4 methods and publish the rank correlation with elite_score; (3) publish the weight rationale — or better, fit the weights so elite_score maximises correlation with (2) and say so. Add a 'Known limitations' section to methodology.md naming the string-match ceiling honestly. Answering issue #38 with 'here are baselines AND here is how well the score tracks real behaviour' is a far stronger launch than baselines alone.
- **[P1 · GAP · L]** Transcripts are 439 tokens of 89% boilerplate — no long-context validity
  - *Evidence:* Measured on buried_constraint_v1_1 with the repo's tokenizer: easy 90 tokens (47.8% distractor), medium 134 (64.9%), hard 233 (79.8%), elite 439 tokens of which 392 (89.3%) are distractors and only 47 tokens are signal. The distractors are drawn from 15 user + 10 assistant fixed strings (src/compactbench/engine/distractors.py:16-45); an elite case's 32 distractor turns contain only 18 distinct strings. The one other difficulty knob, `paraphrase_depth`, is a documented no-op — `paraphrase_depth_for` (engine/difficulty.py:28-34) returns the number and engine/transcript.py never applies any transform (its own docstring says "Actual paraphrasing is a no-op in v1"). So 'elite difficulty' means 'more copies of the same 18 filler sentences'.
  - *Why:* Compaction matters at 50k-500k tokens, where models lose middle context, and none of that regime is exercised. It also invalidates the compression metric: a perfect lossless extractor gets 439/47 = 9.3x, so the Elite-Aggressive 8x floor is clearable with zero information loss, and the compression_bonus term (10% of elite_score) is really 'how much boilerplate did you delete'. A practitioner comparing compaction methods for a real agent will not accept a 439-token benchmark as evidence.
  - *Fix:* Add a long-context suite: generate 20k-100k-token transcripts by interleaving realistic multi-topic content (a bounded corpus of code review, planning and debugging turns is enough — a much larger, non-repeating distractor pool, plus position randomisation of the critical turn across the transcript). Report score-vs-position and score-vs-length curves. Implement `paraphrase_depth` (restate the constraint in progressively more indirect language) or delete the knob from the DSL and docs so difficulty is not overstated. Keep the current toy suite as `starter` for fast iteration and make clear in methodology.md that ranked results come from the long-context suite.
- **[P1 · RISK · S]** Turn tags label the answer inside the compactor's input
  - *Evidence:* `Turn.tags` (contracts/case.py:20-26) carries `critical_constraint`, `decision_override` and `distractor` (engine/transcript.py:59 copies template tags; engine/distractors.py:68 tags every distractor), and the whole `Transcript` object — tags included — is handed to `compactor.compact()` (runner/cycle.py:79). Built-in compactors happen to drop them (`render_transcript`, compactors/_utils.py:16-22), but the LangChain and LlamaIndex adapters go out of their way to preserve them: integrations/langchain.py:96-99 and integrations/llamaindex.py:96-99 copy `compactbench_turn_tags` into `additional_kwargs`. A submitted method can score near-perfectly with `[t for t in transcript.turns if t.tags and 'distractor' not in t.tags]` — no LLM call, no cost, huge compression ratio.
  - *Why:* This is a one-line oracle available to every submitter, and it is documented as a feature in the integration adapters. It will be found the moment there is a leaderboard worth gaming, and it invalidates every entry retroactively because there is no way to tell from results.jsonl whether a method read the tags.
  - *Fix:* Strip tags from the transcript handed to the compactor: build a redacted `Transcript` in cycle.py before calling `compact()` and keep the tagged original for scoring only. Remove tag propagation from both integration adapters (or propagate only turn ids). Add a test asserting every turn passed to a compactor has `tags == []`.
- **[P2 · GAP · S]** Template validator does not validate the scoring spec — bad checks fail mid-run, empty values score free points
  - *Evidence:* `validate_template` (src/compactbench/dsl/validator.py:28-33) checks variable declarations, duplicate keys and reference resolution — nothing about `expected`. An unknown `check` value is only caught by `run_check` (scoring/checks.py:89-92) at scoring time, i.e. after a full run's API spend; run.py:175-178 then cancels every task and propagates, killing the run. An empty `value` auto-passes: `contains_normalized("", ...)` returns 1.0 (checks.py:25-26), `forbidden_absent("", ...)` returns 1.0 (checks.py:32-33), `set_match([], ...)` returns 1.0 (checks.py:44-45) — all three locked in by tests (tests/unit/test_scoring_checks.py:32-33). Nothing checks that the expected value actually occurs in the generated transcript, so an unanswerable item is undetectable. `exact` (checks.py:37-39) is case-sensitive full-string equality against free-form LLM output and is used by zero templates; `set_match` is likewise unused.
  - *Why:* The hidden ranked set is authored under the same validator and cannot be reviewed by the community, so mechanical validation is the only quality gate that exists for it. A silently auto-passing item inflates every method's score equally, which hides real differences.
  - *Fix:* Extend validate_template to: reject unknown check types against a canonical registry; reject empty/whitespace `value` and empty `values`; require `values` for set_match; assert (post-generation, in a test over all templates x N seeds) that every `contains_normalized` value literally occurs in the generated transcript and every `forbidden_absent` value does not occur in its own prompt. Either give `exact` a use or delete it — as written it can only ever score 0 against a chat model.
- **[P2 · IMPROVEMENT · M]** Tokenizer is hardcoded, the config knob is dead, and reasoning-model output breaks the graders
  - *Evidence:* `compression.py:19-31` hardcodes cl100k_base; `Settings.tokenizer` (config.py:32) is read by nothing (grep for 'tokenizer' across src/ returns only the definition, the docstrings and estimate.py:60). On the current English toy transcripts the tokenizer choice barely matters — I measured cl100k vs o200k on a real elite case at a -0.2% ratio difference — so the fixed-yardstick argument in compression.py:3-5 holds for *comparability*; what does not hold is docs/methodology.md:78 implying the ratio reflects savings on the target model. It does not: the leaderboard pins Llama 3.3 70B (decisions.md D6) whose tokenizer is neither. Separately, `CompletionRequest.max_tokens` defaults to 2048 (providers/base.py:38) and no code path ever inspects `finish_reason` — it is captured into `raw` (groq.py:102, openai.py:116) and dropped — so a truncated answer is scored as a real answer. And a reasoning model returning chain-of-thought (exactly the gap PR #37's author flags for vLLM) will restate the forbidden phrase while reasoning about it, scoring 0 on every `forbidden_absent` item and spiking contradiction_rate past the 0.10 qualification floor.
  - *Why:* The moment PR #37 merges and people point CompactBench at vLLM/OpenRouter reasoning models — which is the whole reason that PR exists and the fastest route to submissions — their runs will be scored on chain-of-thought and come back nonsensically bad. That converts new interest into bug reports.
  - *Fix:* (1) Wire `Settings.tokenizer` into `compression.count_tokens` or delete the setting. (2) Report a second `target_model_compression_ratio` using the target's own tokenizer when available, and reword methodology.md:74-79 to say the cl100k ratio is a comparability yardstick, not a context-savings figure. (3) Strip reasoning blocks (`<think>...</think>` and provider reasoning fields) in `evaluate_items` before scoring, and record `finish_reason` on ItemScore, scoring a truncated response as an error rather than a wrong answer. (4) Merge PR #37 with the `extra_body`/`chat_template_kwargs` passthrough its author identified.
- **[P2 · GAP · S]** Family floor silently skips single-family runs; `hard` suite is empty
  - *Evidence:* `qualify` applies the per-family 0.40 guard only when `len(family_means) > 1` (leaderboard/qualification.py:64-72), so a run covering one family is exempt — while docs/elite-program.md:72 promises "Methods must maintain >= 0.40 on every family — a specialist that collapses on one family cannot win overall." Nothing in `qualify` requires that a ranked run covered all required families at all. Separately, decisions.md B8 specifies a public Hard suite of 30 cases and benchmarks/public/hard/ contains only .gitkeep — `compactbench run -s hard` fails with "no templates in suite 'hard'". benchmarks/samples/ and docs/benchmark-governance/ are likewise empty placeholders.
  - *Why:* The family guard is the documented defence against specialist gaming and it is the check that (correctly) rejects the null compactor from finding #1 — leaving a bypass in it removes the one working anti-gaming control. The empty `hard` suite is a documented feature that errors when used, which is exactly what a curious new user from issue #38 will hit.
  - *Fix:* Add a required-families list to the suite metadata and disqualify any ranked run missing one; apply the 0.40 floor unconditionally. Either author the 30-case Hard suite or remove it from decisions.md B8 and the docs; delete the empty benchmarks/samples and docs/benchmark-governance placeholders or fill them.
- **[P2 · STRATEGY · M]** Composite elite_score is not defensible as constructed
  - *Evidence:* elite_score = 0.40*overall + 0.30*drift + 0.20*constraint + 0.10*compression_bonus (leaderboard/ranking.py:44-47). Its four terms are not independent: constraint_retention is computed from a subset of the very item scores that produce overall_score (persistence.py:248-256), so constraint items are counted twice — once at weight 3 inside overall and again at 0.20 of the composite. drift_resistance is ~constant 1.0 in practice (finding #3) so its 0.30 mostly adds a constant. compression_bonus (ranking.py:21-30) is 0 at the floor and saturates at 2x the floor, so an Elite-Light method at 4.1x and one at 40x both receive the full 0.10. Net effect on the measured null compactor: 0.30 + 0.10 = 0.40 of its 0.608 came from the two terms that reward doing nothing.
  - *Why:* The composite is the number the leaderboard sorts on and the number people will quote. Double-counting plus a near-constant term plus a saturating term means the ranking is driven by less signal than the formula suggests, and small elite_score gaps are not meaningful (see finding #9).
  - *Fix:* After fixing findings #1/#3/#5, re-derive the composite: drop constraint_retention from the composite and instead publish it as a separate qualification gate (e.g. constraint_retention >= 0.90 to rank), so it is not double-counted; make compression a continuous log-scaled term rather than a saturating bonus; and keep drift only once it actually varies. Publish the rationale for the final weights in methodology.md next to the validity study, and bump SCORER_VERSION — the leaderboard already segments by scorer_version (projection.py:88-95), so a clean break is cheap while there are zero entries. Doing this now, before any submission exists, costs nothing; doing it after costs every published number.

### Runner & providers

*Failure paths, retries, reproducibility — 23 findings*

The happy path is clean and well-tested (479 tests, deterministic SHA-256 case generation, correct per-case CountingProvider isolation under concurrency), but every failure path is unhandled: I reproduced a run where a single simulated provider 500 destroyed all 12 cases and left only a `run_start` line on disk. Combined with a ~6-second total retry budget, no jitter, no Retry-After, and no request-rate limiter, the two free-tier providers the docs recommend (Groq 100k TPD, Gemini ~15 RPM) essentially cannot complete a run — which is a concrete, mechanical explanation for issue #38's "not a single bench run". Separately, `.env` is documented as the way to supply keys and is silently ignored by every provider; `--estimate` under-counts API calls by 20-100% (measured: 216 projected vs 437 actual) and prints a false "fits within the free-tier daily cap"; and the prompt-caching feature shipped in PR #28's predecessor is a measured no-op because the cached prefix maxes out at ~615 tokens against a 1024-token provider minimum. Nothing that would make a published number reproducible — library version, resolved model snapshot, suite digest, seed, temperature — is recorded in `results.jsonl`.

- **[P0 · BUG · M]** One failed provider call aborts the entire run and discards every completed case
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/runner/run.py:165-178. Cases are fanned out as `asyncio.create_task`, consumed via `asyncio.as_completed`, and the first exception from any task propagates out of the loop, cancels all siblings, and unwinds `run_experiment` — so `RunEndEvent` is never written and nothing in-flight is persisted. There is no try/except around `_run_one` (run.py:139-163) and no per-case error isolation anywhere. I reproduced this with an injected provider that raises on its 25th call, running 12 cases at `--concurrency 4`: the resulting results.jsonl contained exactly one line, `run_start`. Every completed case's API spend was lost. The cancelled tasks are never awaited after `t.cancel()` (run.py:176-177), so in-flight HTTP requests are torn down abruptly and asyncio emits 'exception was never retrieved' noise. The same path fires when a user's compactor raises, when `parse_state` produces >50 warnings and `CompactionArtifact` fails validation, or when `run_check` raises `ScoringError` on a malformed template (scoring/checks.py:57-58). There is no test anywhere in tests/unit/test_runner_run.py or test_runner_cycle.py that exercises a provider or compactor raising mid-run.
  - *Why:* The whole premise of the benchmark is that outsiders plug their own code into it and run it against a paid API for an hour or more. Right now one transient 500 at minute 55 costs the user the entire run and every dollar spent, and `--resume` restarts from zero because nothing was written. This is the single most likely reason a first-time user tries CompactBench once and never comes back, and it is exactly what alexander-potemkin is asking about in issue #38.
  - *Fix:* Wrap `_run_one`'s body in try/except: on failure, write a new `CaseFailedEvent(case_id, template_key, error_type, message)` to the writer and continue. Replace `as_completed`+cancel with `asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)` or keep as_completed but never let an exception escape the loop. Add a `--max-case-failures N` (default e.g. 10% of cases) that stops scheduling new cases once exceeded, then still writes `RunEndEvent` with `notes=['aborted after N case failures']` and a `failed_case_count` field. Make `qualify()` reject any run with failed cases. Add tests: provider raises on call K, compactor raises, compactor returns an invalid artifact.
- **[P0 · RISK · M]** Retry budget is ~6 seconds with no jitter and no Retry-After, and there is no request-rate limiter
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/providers/_retry.py:50-81. `max_retries=3` means at most 2 sleeps of `min(2.0 * 2**attempt, 30.0)` = 2s then 4s — a 6-second total budget before the exception is re-raised (and per finding #1, kills the run). There is no random jitter, so under `--concurrency 4..32` all concurrent retries re-collide in lockstep. The `Retry-After` / `retry-after-ms` header that Groq, OpenAI and Anthropic all return on 429 is never read; `retry_with_backoff` has no access to the exception's response headers. The only concurrency control is `asyncio.Semaphore(concurrency)` over whole *cases* (run.py:136-137) — nothing limits requests per minute. With concurrency 4 and ~1.5s mean latency the runner issues ~160 req/min; Gemini 2.0 Flash's free tier (which docs/getting-started.md:34 recommends) is ~15 RPM and Groq's llama-3.3-70b free tier is 30 RPM. Every provider's `max_retries`/`base_backoff_seconds` constructor args are unreachable from the CLI because `_instantiate_provider` (run.py:277-280) calls `cls()` with no kwargs.
  - *Why:* The default `compactbench run --provider google-ai-studio` or `--provider groq` command in the getting-started guide will 429 within seconds, exhaust the 6-second budget inside the same rate-limit minute, and (per finding #1) lose the whole run. That is a mechanical, reproducible reason nobody has ever published a number. The official ranked-evaluation workflow (.github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml:136-144) runs with these same defaults against Groq/Gemini secrets, so submissions would fail the same way.
  - *Fix:* In `_retry.py`: add full jitter (`delay = random.uniform(0, min(base * 2**attempt, max_delay))`), raise defaults to `max_retries=6, max_delay=60`, and accept an optional `retry_after: Callable[[Exception], float | None]` that each provider implements by reading `exc.response.headers.get('retry-after')`. Add a shared token-bucket `RateLimiter(rpm, tpm)` in `providers/base.py` that every provider awaits before `_call()`, configured per provider key with free-tier-safe defaults. Expose `--requests-per-minute`, `--max-retries` and `--timeout` on the CLI and thread them into `_instantiate_provider` as kwargs. Set `--concurrency 2` as the default until the limiter exists.
- **[P0 · BUG · S]** `.env` is the documented way to supply API keys and is silently ignored by every provider
  - *Evidence:* docs/getting-started.md:53 says "Put keys in `.env` at the project root, or export them in your shell." `src/compactbench/config.py:11-38` defines a pydantic-settings `Settings` class with `env_file='.env'`, but `Settings`/`settings` is imported nowhere — `grep -rn 'from compactbench.config import' src/` returns only `cli.py:16` importing `default_benchmarks_dir`. Every provider reads `os.environ` directly: anthropic.py:41, openai.py:40, groq.py:40, google_ai_studio.py:41, ollama.py:41-43. pydantic-settings' `.env` loading does not populate `os.environ`. I verified this: with a `.env` containing `COMPACTBENCH_GROQ_API_KEY=gsk_fake123`, `Settings().groq_api_key` returns `gsk_fake123` but `'COMPACTBENCH_GROQ_API_KEY' in os.environ` is False and `GroqProvider()` raises `ProviderError: Groq API key required`. `Settings` also has no `anthropic_api_key` or `openai_api_key` field at all, so even wiring it up would not cover the two credit-based providers the docs steer paying users toward.
  - *Why:* This is step one of the getting-started guide. A new user follows the documented instruction, gets 'API key required', and concludes the tool is broken — with no hint that the shell-export path would have worked. Compounded with finding #4 (the error arrives as a raw traceback), the first ninety seconds of the CompactBench experience are a wall.
  - *Fix:* Add `anthropic_api_key`, `openai_api_key`, `openai_base_url` to `Settings` (config.py:21-24) and have every provider resolve its key as `api_key or getattr(settings, '<x>_api_key', None) or os.environ.get(...)`. Alternatively call `load_dotenv()` once in `cli.main`. Add a test that writes a `.env` in tmp_path, chdirs, and asserts each provider constructs. Then either delete the unused `Settings` class or make it the single resolution path.
- **[P1 · BUG · M]** `compactbench run --estimate` under-counts API calls by 20-100% and prints a false free-tier all-clear
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/runner/estimate.py:106 computes `total_calls += cycles_per_case * (1 + len(case.evaluation_items))` — it assumes exactly one compaction call per cycle and omits the drift-cycle continuation call entirely (runner/continuation.py:63 issues one `provider.complete` per drift cycle). `hierarchical_summary.py:57-96` makes `ceil(turns/10) + 1 + 1` calls per cycle and `hybrid_ledger.py:50-64` makes 2. Measured on `--suite elite_practice --case-count 1 --drift-cycles 2`: estimate says **216 API calls** for both methods; the actual run reports `call_count` **256** for naive-summary (the 40 missing continuation calls) and **437** for hierarchical-summary (2.02x). Eval input is also modelled as `transcript_tokens // 4` (~50 tokens for these ~202-token transcripts) plus 80 overhead, when the real cached prefix measures 615 tokens for a generous artifact and 98 tokens for an empty one (evaluation.py:47-73).
