- **[P2 · IMPROVEMENT · M]** Dead parameters, dead modules, and stale scaffolding a new lead should clear early
  - *Evidence:* Concrete items, each verified: (1) `Compactor.compact(config=...)` (`compactors/base.py:31`) is never passed — `cycle.py:79` calls `compact(transcript, previous_artifact=...)` only — yet every submitter must type the parameter, and `submissions/_template/config.yaml:22` exposes a `method_config: {}` knob that therefore does nothing. (2) `src/compactbench/utils/__init__.py` is a docstring-only package promising "tokenizer wrapper, structured logging" that does not exist. (3) `scripts/rebuild_leaderboard.py:166-169` `_ensure_tier` sits after `raise SystemExit(main())`, is unreachable, is documented as "unused at runtime", and uses mypy-syntax `# type: ignore[return-value]`. (4) `run.py:182` re-imports `iter_events` inside the function though `persistence` is already imported at lines 29-39. (5) `run.py:97` `# type: ignore[arg-type]` for `mode: str` where `Literal["write","append"]` is the correct annotation — and a bare `type: ignore` suppresses *all* pyright errors on that line. (6) `cli.py:3-4` claims "Each command raises NotImplementedError until its backing workorder lands" — none do. (7) `CONTRIBUTING.md:19-25` directs new contributors to issues #5/#6/#7 to build WO-008 (submission workflow + leaderboard site), which already exists under `src/compactbench/leaderboard/` and `.github/workflows/`. (8) `dsl/parser.py:141` globs `*.yaml` only, so a contributor's `template.yml` is silently ignored with no error. (9) `_MAX_STRING_LENGTH`/`_MAX_SECTION_ITEMS` in `_state_parser.py:257-258` duplicate the 500/200 bounds in `contracts/artifact.py:141,152-157`. (10) `transcript_to_messages` (`integrations/langchain.py:100-106`) uses `match` with no `case _`, so any future `TurnRole` silently drops turns.
  - *Why:* Individually trivial; collectively they are the signal a stranger reads when deciding whether to invest. CONTRIBUTING pointing at already-completed work is actively harmful — it is the doc a would-be contributor reads first, and it tells them to build something that exists. The dead `config`/`method_config` pair is worse than noise: a submitter fills it in and silently gets nothing.
  - *Fix:* One cleanup PR: delete `utils/`, delete `_ensure_tier` (replacing `_VALID_TIERS` with `set(get_args(CompressionTier))`), delete the duplicate import, fix the `mode` annotation to `Literal`, rewrite the `cli.py` module docstring, glob `*.y*ml`, derive the parser bounds from the pydantic model, add `case _: raise` to both adapters. Decide on `config`: either thread `method_config` from config.yaml through `RunArgs` into `compact(config=...)`, or remove the parameter from the ABC and the key from the template. Rewrite the CONTRIBUTING "Open workorders" section against the actual current backlog — and make that same PR the one that answers issue #38 and reviews PR #37, so the repo's revival is visible from the outside on the same day.

### Project health

*Process, governance, bus factor — 19 findings*

CompactBench is a well-engineered repo with a governance story that exists almost entirely on paper. The machinery a benchmark needs to be trusted — a release cadence, a working submission gate, a pinned ranked protocol, a named arbiter, a second maintainer — is either undone, unprovisioned, or contradicted by the code. Concretely: 17 merged PRs (two new providers, two framework integrations, a whole new template family) have sat unreleased since 2026-04-19 with an empty `[Unreleased]` CHANGELOG section; the `evaluate` label that the entire submission pipeline is gated on does not exist in the repo; PR #37 has never had a single CI check run because fork workflows need first-time-contributor approval nobody gave; and the repo has exactly one collaborator (`UsernameLoad`, admin) so the bus factor is literally 1. The fix set is mostly small, mechanical automation plus one hard editorial decision: publish an honest, narrower scope than the docs currently promise, and delete the promises you will not keep.

- **[P0 · STALE · M]** 17 merged PRs of user-visible features have been unreleased for 4 months; CHANGELOG [Unreleased] is empty
  - *Evidence:* `git log v0.1.0..HEAD` = 17 commits from 2026-04-17 to 2026-04-19, +1917/-61 lines in src/ alone: Anthropic + OpenAI providers (src/compactbench/providers/anthropic.py, openai.py, PR #19), LangChain (#16) and LlamaIndex (#17) integrations, prompt caching (#23), case-level parallelism + `--estimate` (#22), TokenUsage telemetry (#28), a fourth template family `reference_resolution` (#27, 5 new files in benchmarks/public/elite_practice/), scoring diagnostics (src/compactbench/scoring/diagnostics.py). CHANGELOG.md:8-9 shows `## [Unreleased]` followed immediately by `## [0.1.0]` — not one of those 17 PRs added an entry, even though .github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md:25 requires it and nothing in ci.yml enforces it. `mcp__github__list_releases` returns exactly one release, v0.1.0 (2026-04-17). README.md:91-97 still describes v0.1.0 as the current state and lists only Groq/Google/Ollama providers.
  - *Why:* Anyone who does the README's `pip install compactbench` gets the April build: no OpenAI provider, no Anthropic provider, no LangChain/LlamaIndex adapters, three families instead of four. jaaabir's PR #37 adds `base_url` to an OpenAI provider that does not exist in any released artifact — the contributor is working against git main because the released package is useless to them. Issue #38 asks 'what to compare with?' from a project whose newest published artifact is 4 months behind its own main branch. A benchmark that cannot ship is not a benchmark anyone will build on.
  - *Fix:* Cut 0.2.0 this week: (1) reconstruct CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` from `git log --oneline v0.1.0..HEAD` (17 entries, ~1 hour of writing), rename it `## [0.2.0] — <date>`, add the compare links; (2) bump `version` in pyproject.toml:3 (note: it is `[project] version`, NOT `[tool.hatch]`); (3) `git tag -s v0.2.0 && git push origin v0.2.0` and approve the `pypi` environment; (4) update README.md:91-99 'Project status' to 0.2.0 and add the new providers/integrations. Then add a CI job `changelog` to .github/workflows/ci.yml that fails a PR touching `src/**` unless CHANGELOG.md is also modified (skip via a `no-changelog` label), so this cannot silently recur.
- **[P0 · RISK · S]** PR #37 has never had a single CI check run — fork workflows are blocked on first-time-contributor approval
  - *Evidence:* `pull_request_read(method=get_check_runs, pullNumber=37)` returns `{"total_count":0,"check_runs":[]}` — zero checks, 18 days after the PR opened from fork `jaaabir/compactbench`. By contrast dependabot PR #31 (same-repo branch) has 11 check runs, all green. `pull_request_read(method=get_reviews, pullNumber=37)` returns `[]`. .github/workflows/ci.yml:3-7 triggers on `pull_request` and needs no secrets, so nothing in the workflow itself blocks it; the near-certain cause is the repo's Actions setting 'Fork pull request workflows from outside collaborators → Require approval for first-time contributors', which holds the run until a maintainer clicks approve. That is also why the lead observed `mergeable_state=blocked`: required checks can never report.
  - *Why:* The one high-quality outside contribution this project has ever received got literally zero signal — no CI, no review, no comment — for 18 days. From the contributor's side the repo looks abandoned and hostile. Every future contributor hits the same wall, and it is invisible from inside the repo because nothing surfaces 'a run is waiting for approval'.
  - *Fix:* Two changes: (1) Settings → Actions → General → set fork-PR approval to 'Require approval for first-time contributors who are new to GitHub' (CI needs no secrets, and evaluate-submission.yml is separately gated on a label, so this is safe); (2) add a scheduled triage workflow `.github/workflows/triage-sla.yml` (cron daily) that queries open PRs/issues with no maintainer response in >3 days and opens/updates a single 'Triage backlog' issue assigned to the maintainer — a self-nagging mechanism that survives a distracted month. Immediately: approve and review #37, it is exactly the kind of contribution the project needs.
- **[P0 · BUG · S]** The `evaluate` label the whole submission pipeline is gated on does not exist in the repository
  - *Evidence:* `get_label(name='evaluate')` → "label 'evaluate' not found in compactbench/compactbench". That label is the authorization gate in .github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml:25 (`if: github.event.label.name == 'evaluate'`), the documented flow in CONTRIBUTING.md:81, docs/submitting.md:9 and decisions.md:231 (E4), and docs/leaderboard.md:42 links visitors to `pulls?q=is%3Apr+label%3Aevaluate` — a query that returns an empty list because the label has never been created. `needs-triage`, `method-submission` and `template-proposal` are also missing (`get_label` 404s), yet all three issue templates declare them (.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug_report.md:5, method_submission.md:6, template_proposal.md:6), so template labels are silently dropped — issue #38 arrived with no labels at all.
  - *Why:* The submission pipeline has never run end to end and the first thing a would-be submitter sees on the leaderboard page is an invitation pointing at an empty label query. Combined with zero submissions ever, the entire ranked mechanism is unproven — and the docs describe it as if it were operational.
  - *Fix:* Add `.github/labels.yml` listing every label the repo's automation and templates reference (`evaluate`, `reevaluate`, `needs-triage`, `bug`, `method-submission`, `template-proposal`, `dependencies`, `ci`, `python`, `good first issue`, `help wanted`) plus `.github/workflows/label-sync.yml` running `EndBug/label-sync` on push to main — labels as code, provisioned automatically. Then dry-run the whole pipeline by submitting a maintainer baseline (see the baselines finding) so the gate is exercised at least once before a stranger depends on it.
- **[P0 · BUG · M]** The ranked evaluation protocol is read from the submitter's own config — seed group, difficulty, drift cycles and case count are all uncontrolled
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml:122-126 lifts `PROVIDER`, `MODEL`, `DRIFT_CYCLES` and `CASE_COUNT` straight out of the submitter's `config.yaml`, and lines 136-144 pass them to `compactbench run` with no allow-list. The run passes no `--seed-group`, so it uses the CLI default `"default"` (src/compactbench/cli.py:68) — not the `elite_v1_q1` group that docs/governance/elite-versions.md:26 says the ranked board pins. It passes no `--difficulty`, so ranked runs execute at `medium` (cli.py:56), while docs/elite-program.md describes elite adversarial load. Worse, scripts/rebuild_leaderboard.py:81-96 reads `drift_cycles` from the same submitter config and hands it to `qualify(..., expected_drift_cycles=drift_cycles)` — so the 'all configured drift cycles completed' floor (decisions.md:118) checks the submitter's run against the submitter's own declared bar. `provider` is unconstrained too: `mock` is a valid value in submissions/_template/config.yaml:10.
  - *Why:* The moment a first entry appears, the published number is not comparable to anything: two submitters can be ranked on different seed groups, difficulties, case counts and cycle depths, and the anti-overfitting seed-rotation policy the FAQ sells (docs/faq.md:52) is not implemented in the pipeline at all. This makes published numbers wrong rather than merely absent — the worst outcome for a benchmark trying to earn trust.
  - *Fix:* Create `src/compactbench/ranked_protocol.py` (or `benchmarks/ranked_protocol.yaml`) holding the maintainer-pinned constants: `suite=elite_v1`, `seed_group=elite_v1_q1`, `difficulty=elite`, `drift_cycles=2`, `case_count_per_template=20`, and an allow-list of ranked `(provider, model)` pairs. Have evaluate-submission.yml pass those explicitly and read from `config.yaml` only `class_path`, `compression_tier` and `attribution`; fail the job if the submission declares a provider/model outside the allow-list. Have rebuild_leaderboard.py import the same constants for `qualify()` instead of trusting config.yaml, and reject any results.jsonl whose recorded `seed_group`/`difficulty`/`drift_cycles` do not match the protocol. Document the protocol table in docs/methodology.md.
- **[P1 · RISK · M]** Bus factor is exactly 1 — one collaborator, no governance doc, no succession plan
  - *Evidence:* `list_repository_collaborators(affiliation=all)` returns a single entry: `{"login":"UsernameLoad","role_name":"admin"}`. .github/CODEOWNERS:5-10 assigns every path to that one account. decisions.md:222-223 (F1) says additional maintainers join via a `compactbench/maintainers` GitHub team that does not appear to exist. SECURITY.md:44 tells the public 'CompactBench is maintained by a small volunteer group' and docs/faq.md:33 says the hidden set is 'curated and rotated by the maintainer team' — there is no group and no team. The same account is the sole path to the org, the private `compactbench/compactbench-hidden` repo, the `HIDDEN_REPO_TOKEN` secret, the PyPI project, and the Pages deployment. There is no GOVERNANCE.md or MAINTAINERS.md at repo root (`ls` of root confirms).
  - *Why:* The 4-month silence is the mild version of this failure; account loss is the terminal version — the hidden ranked set, the only thing making the leaderboard defensible, becomes unrecoverable and every published rank becomes unreproducible. Labs and researchers evaluating whether to submit will check exactly this before investing.
  - *Fix:* Write `GOVERNANCE.md` at repo root (link it from README and mkdocs nav) covering: named maintainers and their scopes; how someone becomes a maintainer; the decision process for benchmark-content changes; dispute/appeal path; conflict-of-interest rule (a maintainer's own method is evaluated by a different maintainer, or clearly labelled 'maintainer baseline, not ranked'); and an explicit continuity clause — a second org owner, a second PyPI owner, and an encrypted copy of the hidden-repo content held by a named backup person. Recruit that second person before publishing baselines; being the only person who can arbitrate is the thing that makes arbitration claims unbelievable. Update SECURITY.md:44 and docs/faq.md:33 to say 'one maintainer' until it is not true.
- **[P1 · BUG · S]** Dependabot's Python config does not match the ecosystem actually in use, so grouping, labels and prefixes are silently ignored
  - *Evidence:* .github/dependabot.yml:4-24 declares `package-ecosystem: "pip"` with a `python-dependencies` group (minor+patch), labels `[dependencies, python]`, commit prefix `deps`. The actual open PRs are on branches `dependabot/uv/urllib3-2.7.0`, `dependabot/uv/idna-3.15`, `dependabot/uv/cryptography-48.0.1`, `dependabot/uv/pymdown-extensions-10.21.3`, `dependabot/uv/pydantic-settings-2.14.2` — the **uv** ecosystem, not pip — carrying labels `[dependencies, python:uv]` and titles prefixed `ci(deps):`. None of the configured grouping, labelling or prefix applied. By contrast the github-actions entry works exactly as configured: PR #34 is titled 'Bump the actions-dependencies group across 1 directory with 4 updates' with labels `[dependencies, ci]`. urllib3/idna/cryptography are not in pyproject.toml at all — they are transitive lockfile entries, i.e. these are ungrouped Dependabot *security* updates, open since 2026-05-11.
  - *Why:* SECURITY.md:31 tells reporters 'we track fixes via Dependabot' while three transitive security bumps (cryptography 46→48, urllib3, idna) have been open and green for three months. And because the config never matched, the maintainer is getting the maximum-noise version of Dependabot — one PR per package — which is exactly what causes the backlog that then gets ignored.
  - *Fix:* Change `package-ecosystem: "pip"` → `"uv"` at .github/dependabot.yml:4, and add a second group entry with `applies-to: security-updates` so security bumps batch too. Add `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml`: on `pull_request` from `dependabot[bot]`, use `dependabot/fetch-metadata` and `gh pr merge --auto --squash` for `version-update:semver-patch` and `semver-minor` (leave majors for human review); enable 'Allow auto-merge' in repo settings and require CI as a status check so auto-merge waits for green. For the current backlog: close #31/#32/#33/#35/#36, land one `uv lock --upgrade` PR, keep #34 (actions group) and merge it.
- **[P1 · STALE · S]** The contributor front door points at three closed issues and there is no open work to pick up
  - *Evidence:* CONTRIBUTING.md:15-23 — the second section anyone reads — is headed 'Open workorders — pickup welcome' and links issues #5 (WO-008), #6 (WO-009, 'good starter issue') and #7 (WO-010) as `help wanted`. All three shipped long ago (commits 8e14694 #9, 0745d30 #10, f794b8e #11) and `list_issues(state=OPEN)` returns `totalCount: 1` — only alexander-potemkin's #38. docs/faq.md:77 routes 'Method submission questions' to 'comment on #5', a closed internal workorder issue, and that page is live on the docs site. README.md:99 claims post-launch work 'is tracked in GitHub issues'; there are none.
  - *Why:* A developer who arrives interested (which is exactly what is happening right now) reads CONTRIBUTING, clicks three dead links, finds an empty issue tracker and leaves. The project currently converts inbound interest into nothing, and the published contact path for the highest-value contribution type — method submissions — is a closed issue.
  - *Fix:* Delete CONTRIBUTING.md:15-23 and replace it with a link to a live `help wanted` / `good first issue` query. File 8-12 real issues from the actual backlog (reference_resolution hidden-set parity, `hard` suite content, extra_body/chat_template_kwargs passthrough that jaaabir flagged, vLLM/OpenAI-compatible docs, baseline runs, labels-as-code, stale-bot, CHANGELOG CI check) and label 3 of them `good first issue` (that label already exists). Fix docs/faq.md:74-79 to route method questions to the issue template and/or GitHub Discussions. Add `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` with `blank_issues_enabled: false` and contact_links to Discussions + docs, and add a fourth template `question.md` — #38 was a question and no template fit it.
- **[P1 · STALE · M]** Governance docs contradict the implementation on almost every operational detail
  - *Evidence:* docs/submitting.md:10 says 'The self-hosted runner executes your method' — decisions.md:186-190 (E1) explicitly dropped self-hosted for GitHub-hosted. decisions.md:196 and :204 (E2/E3) say the hidden repo is cloned 'using a deploy key stored as a repo secret'; the workflow actually uses a fine-grained PAT (`HIDDEN_REPO_TOKEN`, evaluate-submission.yml:98-106, changed in commit e525799 'switch hidden-repo auth from deploy key to fine-grained PAT') and its own comment says org policy blocks deploy keys. docs/submitting.md:54 documents a `reevaluate` label for resubmissions, but evaluate-submission.yml:25 only matches `evaluate` — re-evaluation as documented is a no-op. docs/governance/elite-versions.md:15-19 says Elite v1 is 3 families / 15 public templates; `compactbench suites list` reports elite_practice = 20 templates across 4 families (reference_resolution added in #27) with no version bump and no changelog entry (elite-versions.md:86-92 still ends at v1.0, 2026-04-17). decisions.md:226 (F2) promises quarterly Elite review; the last one was the 2026-04-17 launch, so the July review is ~2 months overdue with nothing recorded.
  - *Why:* The Elite program's whole pitch (docs/elite-program.md:9) is 'maintained as a program so the claim can hold'. Published governance that does not match the code is worse than no governance: a reviewer who checks two claims and finds both stale concludes the hidden-set and anti-gaming claims are equally decorative.
  - *Fix:* One doc-truthing PR: fix submitting.md:10 (GitHub-hosted), decisions.md:196/204 (PAT + rotation owner), and either implement `reevaluate` in evaluate-submission.yml:25 (`contains(fromJSON('["evaluate","reevaluate"]'), github.event.label.name)`) or delete it from submitting.md:54. Then cut Elite v1.1 in docs/governance/elite-versions.md: add reference_resolution to the active-family table, state the public/hidden parity status for that family honestly, and append a changelog entry. Add a CI check that any diff touching `benchmarks/**` also touches `docs/governance/elite-versions.md` — the cheapest possible enforcement of the version policy the doc already promises.
- **[P1 · GAP · M]** The benchmark has no first-class version; suite_version is inferred from template files and can silently be "mixed"
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/runner/run.py:283-287 derives `suite_version` as the single distinct `template.version` across the suite, falling back to the literal string `"mixed"` when they differ (duplicated at src/compactbench/cli.py:164-165). Every template file declares `version: 1.0.0` (grep over benchmarks/public/elite_practice/*.yaml), including the five reference_resolution files added in #27 — so adding a whole new family did not change `suite_version`. src/compactbench/leaderboard/projection.py:68 stamps `benchmark_version = f"{suite_key}@{suite_version}"`, and docs/leaderboard.md:54 groups rows by that value. decisions.md:229 (F3) and elite-versions.md:41 require a new leaderboard version whenever the Elite version bumps.
  - *Why:* Runs scored on a 3-family suite and runs scored on a 4-family suite both stamp `elite_v1@1.0.0` and land in the same leaderboard group — silently incomparable numbers presented as a ranking. And if one template ever gets a patch bump, every future run is labelled `elite_v1@mixed`, which is not a version at all. The reproducibility guarantee in README.md:20 ('every result is stamped with suite / scorer / model / method versions') is not actually backed by a suite version anyone controls.
  - *Fix:* Add a suite manifest `benchmarks/public/<suite>/suite.yaml` with `suite_key`, `suite_version`, `seed_group`, and the expected family list; make `load_suite` require it, stamp `RunStartEvent.suite_version` from it, and delete `_suite_version()`/the `"mixed"` fallback (fail loudly instead). Add a CI guard: any change under `benchmarks/**` must bump `suite_version` in that manifest. Cross-link the manifest's `suite_version` to the Elite version in docs/governance/elite-versions.md so package version (0.2.0), scorer version (1.0.0) and benchmark version (elite_v1.1) are three explicitly separate, documented numbers.
- **[P1 · GAP · S]** No published response-time policy, and the one SLA that exists is already broken
  - *Evidence:* SECURITY.md:14-16 promises 'Acknowledgement within 3 business days' and 'Substantive response within 14 days' — while issue #38 sat unanswered and PR #37 sat unreviewed for 18 days, in a repo whose last commit is 2026-04-19. SECURITY.md:47 then says 'We cannot offer ... guaranteed response SLAs', contradicting the table above it. CONTRIBUTING.md contains no response-time expectation at all, and docs/submitting.md gives no turnaround for submission evaluation — the highest-stakes wait in the project.
  - *Why:* A submitter is being asked to write a method, open a PR, and wait an unspecified time for a maintainer to hand-apply a label and run a job. With a visible 4-month gap in the commit history and no stated expectation, the rational choice is not to bother. An honest, modest, met SLA converts more contributors than an ambitious, broken one.
  - *Fix:* Add a 'What to expect from maintainers' section to CONTRIBUTING.md with numbers you can actually hit as one person: first response to issues/PRs within 7 days; submission evaluation labelled within 14 days; a monthly triage sweep on the first Monday. Reconcile SECURITY.md:14-16 with :47 (keep the security ack at 3 days, drop the contradiction). Back the promise with the triage-SLA cron workflow from the fork-PR finding, plus a `send_later`-style calendar reminder — the mechanism, not the intention, is what makes this survive month three.
- **[P1 · RISK · S]** The hidden-set PAT is an unmonitored expiring credential with no rotation runbook; docs/runbooks/ is empty
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml:95-107 clones the hidden repo with `HIDDEN_REPO_TOKEN`, a fine-grained PAT (per its own comment and commit e525799), and hard-fails when the secret is absent — correctly refusing to fall back to public cases. Fine-grained PATs expire (1 year maximum). `docs/runbooks/` contains only `.gitkeep`, as does `docs/benchmark-governance/`; neither is in the mkdocs nav (mkdocs.yml:61-75). There is no documented owner, expiry date, or rotation procedure for that token anywhere in the repo (grep for HIDDEN_REPO_TOKEN finds only the workflow).
  - *Why:* The first submission may well arrive after the token silently expires — the submitter then gets a red job and an error telling them to ask a maintainer to fix a secret they cannot see. It is also the single credential guarding the hidden ranked set, held by the one account, with no rotation record for any future co-maintainer to inherit.
  - *Fix:* Write `docs/runbooks/hidden-set-access.md` (token scope: Contents:Read on compactbench-hidden only; owner; expiry date; rotation steps; who to contact) and `docs/runbooks/release.md`, `docs/runbooks/leaderboard-rebuild.md`, `docs/runbooks/leak-response.md`; add a Runbooks section to mkdocs.yml nav or delete the empty placeholder directories. Add a monthly scheduled workflow that calls the hidden repo with the token and opens an issue if the call fails — expiry then announces itself instead of ambushing the first real submission.
- **[P1 · GAP · M]** No enforcement or dispute process behind the anti-cheating claims — 'banned from the board' with no arbiter or appeal
  - *Evidence:* submissions/README.md:34 states 'Results that rely on hidden-case exposure are removed and the submitter is banned from the board.' Nothing defines who decides, on what evidence, whether the accused sees the evidence, whether there is an appeal, or where bans are recorded. decisions.md:234 (F5) says flagged submissions 'are reviewed by a maintainer' with heuristics 'documented privately'. docs/governance/elite-versions.md:73-75 says shadow evaluation is manual and applied 'by hand'. There is no conflict-of-interest rule anywhere for a maintainer submitting their own method, and there is exactly one maintainer (`list_repository_collaborators` → 1 admin), so accuser, arbiter and appellate body are the same person.
  - *Why:* Benchmarks live or die on adjudication credibility. The first time a real dispute happens — and it will, because the pipeline reads submitter-controlled knobs — the project has no process to point at, and the maintainer's own baseline entries will be on the same board they arbitrate.
  - *Fix:* Add a 'Disputes and enforcement' section to the new GOVERNANCE.md: what evidence triggers review; that the submitter is notified and gets 14 days to respond in the PR; that outcomes (warning / entry removed / ban) are recorded publicly in `docs/governance/enforcement-log.md` with the reasoning; that a ban is time-boxed and appealable to a second maintainer once one exists. Add the COI rule: maintainer-authored methods are published as 'baseline (maintainer)' and excluded from ranked position, or reviewed by a different maintainer. Keeping the heuristics private is fine; keeping the *process* private is not.
- **[P1 · IMPROVEMENT · M]** Zero process automation: no stale bot, no auto-merge, no scheduled CI, no release drafting, no CHANGELOG gate
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/ contains exactly six files: ci.yml, codeql.yml, docs.yml, evaluate-submission.yml, release.yml, update-leaderboard.yml. ci.yml:3-7 runs only on push/PR to main — there is no `schedule:` trigger, so during the 4-month silence nothing ever verified main still built against current dependencies (codeql.yml:8-9 is the only cron in the repo). No auto-merge workflow, no stale/rot workflow, no auto-assign to CODEOWNERS, no release-drafter, and nothing enforcing the PR template's CHANGELOG checkbox (.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md:25).
  - *Why:* Every one of the four failures the lead identified — silent staleness, unanswered issue, unreviewed PR, dependabot backlog — is a task a bot does for free while a single maintainer is busy. Automation is the only sustainable answer to 'how does this not happen again'; a resolution to be more attentive is not.
  - *Fix:* Add, in this order (each is ~20 lines): (1) `schedule: - cron: '0 6 * * 1'` to ci.yml so weekly green/red is visible even with zero commits; (2) `.github/workflows/dependabot-auto-merge.yml` (see the Dependabot finding); (3) `.github/workflows/triage-sla.yml` — daily sweep opening/updating one backlog issue for anything unanswered >7 days; (4) `.github/workflows/label-sync.yml` + `.github/labels.yml`; (5) a `changelog` job in ci.yml gating `src/**` changes; (6) release-drafter or a `workflow_dispatch` 'prepare release' job that assembles the CHANGELOG section from merged PR titles. Skip a classic stale-bot that closes issues — with one open issue it would only be rude; the triage sweep is the version this project needs.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · M]** Publish maintainer baseline runs — it is the answer to issue #38 and the only way to prove the pipeline works
  - *Evidence:* docs/data/leaderboard.json is `{"entries": [], "updated_at": "2026-04-17T00:00:00+00:00"}`; submissions/ contains only `_template/`. Four built-in compactors exist and are documented as baselines (README.md:35, docs/leaderboard.md:45-46 'the four built-in compactors give you a baseline to beat'), but no baseline number has ever been published. Issue #38 (2026-08-23, alexander-potemkin) is precisely: 'not a single run result — why is that? What to compare with?'. Meanwhile the publication chain has never executed: update-leaderboard.yml:43-53 pushes directly to main, and scripts/rebuild_leaderboard.py:126-129 mentions branch protection rejecting no-op commits — that push has never been tested with a real entry.
  - *Why:* An empty leaderboard is a benchmark that has never benchmarked anything. It is also the only fact an outsider needs to decide the project is not real. Baselines simultaneously answer #38, give submitters a target, and force the ranked pipeline (label gate, hidden clone, results commit, leaderboard rebuild, Pages redeploy) to run end to end while the only person who gets embarrassed by a failure is the maintainer.
  - *Fix:* Run all four built-ins against the ranked protocol on the pinned model, commit them under `submissions/compactbench-baselines/<method>/` with `attribution.org: 'CompactBench (maintainer baseline)'`, and label them as baselines in the leaderboard projection so they are visibly non-competing. Publish them as part of 0.2.0 and reply to #38 with the numbers plus the run command so it is reproducible. Then add a runbook + scheduled job that refreshes baselines every release, so 'the board is empty' can never be the state again.
- **[P2 · BUG · S]** Release workflow is sound but its runbook is wrong and it cannot be rehearsed
  - *Evidence:* .github/workflows/release.yml uses PyPI trusted publishing via OIDC (lines 30-32, 86-87, `environment: pypi`) with a tag/version consistency check (57-66) and SHA-pinned actions — the design is right. But the header runbook at line 21 says 'Bump [tool.hatch] version in pyproject.toml and __init__.py': there is no `[tool.hatch] version` (the version lives at pyproject.toml:3 under `[project]`) and src/compactbench/__init__.py:6 reads the version from installed metadata, so there is nothing to bump there. The workflow triggers only on `push: tags` (line 27-28) — no `workflow_dispatch`, no TestPyPI path, so the first thing that exercises the release path after 4 months is a real, irreversible tag push.
  - *Why:* The person about to cut 0.2.0 after a 4-month gap will follow a runbook with two wrong steps, and has no way to rehearse. A failed publish leaves a permanent tag and a GitHub release pointing at nothing, which is a bad first impression for a project whose credibility problem is already 'nothing ships'.
  - *Fix:* Fix release.yml:21 to name `pyproject.toml [project] version` only. Add `workflow_dispatch` with a `dry_run` boolean input that builds, runs the version check, and publishes to TestPyPI (`repository-url: https://test.pypi.org/legacy/`) while skipping the `github-release` job. Before tagging 0.2.0, confirm the `pypi` deployment environment and the PyPI trusted-publisher entry still exist (they were configured for v0.1.0 in April; verify pypi.org/project/compactbench actually shows 0.1.0).
- **[P2 · IMPROVEMENT · S]** Submission validation and the honesty rules only run behind the maintainer-gated workflow
  - *Evidence:* scripts/validate_submissions.py (FILL_ME placeholder check) is invoked only from .github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml:48-49, which requires the `evaluate` label. ci.yml has no submissions job, so a PR adding `submissions/<handle>/<method>/` gets no automated feedback at all until a human acts. The validator also checks only for `FILL_ME` — nothing verifies that `config.yaml` has the required keys `method.class_path`, `runtime.provider/model/compression_tier`, `attribution.handle` (all of which evaluate-submission.yml:114-127 and rebuild_leaderboard.py:72-94 will later index into), that `class_path` points at a file that exists, or that the class subclasses `Compactor`.
  - *Why:* The submission path is the project's product. Right now it silently does nothing until a maintainer notices, which multiplies the maintainer's per-submission cost and gives the submitter no way to self-serve their way to a valid PR — the exact combination that made PR #37 rot.
  - *Fix:* Add a `submissions` job to ci.yml that runs on any PR touching `submissions/**`: `validate_submissions.py`, a new schema check of `config.yaml` (pydantic model mirroring what the workflow and rebuild script consume), an import check that `class_path` resolves and subclasses `Compactor`, and a smoke run of the method against `--suite starter --provider mock --case-count 1`. That way a submitter arrives at the maintainer's label gate already green, and the maintainer's job shrinks to reading the method and clicking one label.
- **[P2 · RISK · S]** Advertised contact and funding channels point at infrastructure that probably does not exist
  - *Evidence:* CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md:50 routes all conduct reports to `conduct@compactbench.dev`. decisions.md:151 (C3) lists the `compactbench.dev` domain as an unmade decision ('Porkbun or Namecheap — post-scaffold decision'), and every live URL in the repo is `compactbench.github.io` (mkdocs.yml:3, README.md:9) — so that mailbox is almost certainly undeliverable. .github/FUNDING.yml:8 sets `github: [UsernameLoad]` (with a comment admitting Sponsors may not be activated) and lines 5-7 direct corporate sponsors to 'the certification-service contact path' at the docs site; grepping the whole repo for 'certification' finds only that comment — no such path exists on the site.
  - *Why:* A Code of Conduct whose only reporting address bounces is not a Code of Conduct, and it is one of the first files a cautious contributor checks. A Sponsor button that 404s and a funding note pointing at a nonexistent commercial offering read as abandonment or vapor to exactly the corporate readers the project wants.
  - *Fix:* Either register compactbench.dev and stand up the alias, or replace CODE_OF_CONDUCT.md:50 with a channel that exists today — GitHub's private vulnerability reporting form is already used by SECURITY.md:8, or a personal address you actually read. Verify GitHub Sponsors is activated for the account; if not, delete .github/FUNDING.yml until it is. Delete the certification-service sentence or write the page it points to.
- **[P2 · IMPROVEMENT · S]** pre-commit pins ruff v0.6.9 while CI runs ruff 0.15.x — contributors get formatting churn
  - *Evidence:* .pre-commit-config.yaml:17 pins `astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit` at `v0.6.9` and :24 pins pyright at `v1.1.383`; the project venv resolves `ruff 0.15.11` (`.venv/bin/ruff --version`) and ci.yml:28-31 runs `uv run ruff check/format --check` against that lockfile version. CONTRIBUTING.md:34 tells every contributor to `pre-commit install`. Nothing runs `pre-commit autoupdate` on a schedule and no workflow runs pre-commit in CI, so the drift is invisible until it bites.
  - *Why:* A contributor's local hook can reformat code in a way CI then rejects (or vice versa) across a 9-minor-version formatter gap — an annoying, confidence-eroding first experience for exactly the drive-by contributors the project is currently failing to convert.
  - *Fix:* Pin the pre-commit ruff rev to the lockfile's ruff version and add a monthly scheduled workflow running `pre-commit autoupdate` that opens a PR (or enable pre-commit.ci, which does autoupdate PRs and runs hooks on every PR for free on public repos). Add `.pre-commit-config.yaml` to the dependabot config so the pins are tracked like any other dependency.
- **[P2 · STRATEGY · M]** Advertised scope exceeds what one maintainer can deliver — the `hard` suite ships empty and several promises are unstaffed
  - *Evidence:* `compactbench suites list` reports `hard | 0 templates | —` because benchmarks/public/hard/ is empty, while decisions.md:134 (B8) specifies 'Hard public (30 cases)' — a user running `--suite hard` hits the 'no templates in suite' error path (cli.py:161-163). decisions.md:225-235 commits to quarterly Elite reviews (overdue), manual shadow evaluation of top entries (elite-versions.md:73), 15 template families (decisions.md:126), and decisions.md:242-253 (Part G) defers eight more workstreams. docs/architecture/workorders.md ends at WO-010; there is no roadmap covering anything after launch, and README.md:99 defers to a GitHub issue tracker that has one issue in it.
  - *Why:* The gap between promised program and delivered program is what the 4-month silence actually measured. Every unkept commitment in a governance doc devalues the ones that are kept, and a shipped-but-empty suite is a broken feature a first-time user can trip over in minute two.
  - *Fix:* Publish `docs/roadmap.md` (add to mkdocs nav) with three honest sections: **Committed** (0.2.0 release, baselines published, submission pipeline exercised end-to-end, Elite v1.1 doc truthing) with dates; **Wanted, help needed** (template families 5-15, shadow-eval automation, extra_body passthrough for reasoning models via vLLM, `hard` suite content) linked to filed issues; **Not doing for now** (custom domain, certification service, paper, non-English content, Dockerized third-party runners) — and delete those from decisions.md Part G or mark them explicitly abandoned. Either populate `benchmarks/public/hard/` or remove the suite from the shipped directory so it stops appearing in `suites list`. Scope honesty is a feature for a single-maintainer project; ambiguity is the thing that reads as abandonment.

### Strategy

*Positioning and what to build next — 14 findings*

CompactBench has a real thesis and a well-engineered skeleton, but it is positioned for a 2025 market and shipped zero evidence. Between April and August 2026 the field converged on exactly this problem and moved past it: Factory.ai published probe-based compaction evals over 36k real production messages with an LLM judge and ranked Anthropic vs OpenAI native compaction; arXiv 2606.22528 (ConstraintRot, June 2026) published the headline result CompactBench was built to produce (constraint violations 0% with full context to 30% after compaction, peak 59%); Letta ships Context-Bench with a live leaderboard; Princeton shipped Slipstream. Meanwhile CompactBench's hardest case is a 450-token transcript scored by substring matching that gives 0.0 to a correct answer and 1.0 to a paraphrased violation, and its leaderboard has been empty for four months. The good news: nobody has published a vendor-neutral head-to-head of the compaction implementations people actually ship (Anthropic compact-2026-01-12, OpenAI's compaction endpoint, Inspect AI's five strategies), and Inspect AI ships five compaction strategies with explicitly zero quality evaluation. That gap plus multi-cycle drift is a defensible position — but only after the scorer and the case sizes are fixed. Do not publish a single number on the current scorer.

- **[P0 · STRATEGY · M]** Empty leaderboard is the adoption blocker, but publishing on the current scorer would be worse than publishing nothing
  - *Evidence:* docs/data/leaderboard.json is {"entries": [], "updated_at": "2026-04-17"}. Zero submissions (only submissions/_template/). Issue #38 (filed today by an outside user) is literally "not a single run result - why is that? What to compare with?". A web search for "CompactBench" returns only the project's own repo — no third-party mention exists anywhere. Meanwhile the scorer that would produce those numbers is demonstrably sign-inverted (see next finding). I ran the full elite_practice suite end-to-end against the mock provider in seconds: the pipeline works, `compactbench score` renders a complete aggregate table (overall_score, drift_resistance, constraint_retention, contradiction_rate, compression_ratio). Nothing but content validity stands between the repo and published baselines.
  - *Why:* A benchmark with no numbers is not a benchmark, it is a library. Every visitor asks the same question #38 asks and leaves. But the first numbers you publish become the thing people cite and the thing that gets audited; shipping numbers from a scorer that scores a correct answer 0.0 would be an unrecoverable credibility event for a project whose entire pitch is measurement discipline.
  - *Fix:* Sequence, do not parallelize: (1) fix the scorer and drift metric (next three findings), (2) add the oracle and null controls, (3) run the four built-ins plus both controls on a current model, (4) publish to docs/data/leaderboard.json and answer #38 with the link and the raw results.jsonl. Target two weeks. Answer #38 TODAY with the honest version ("the runner works, the scorer has a validity bug I am fixing, baselines land <date>") — a dated commitment to an interested stranger is worth more than silence.
- **[P0 · BUG · M]** Lexical scoring inverts the sign on the flagship metric: a correct, compliant answer scores 0.0 and counts as a contradiction
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/scoring/checks.py:30-34 `forbidden_absent` returns 0.0 if the forbidden phrase appears anywhere in the response. src/compactbench/scoring/contradictions.py:36-42 `response_violates` uses the same substring test. benchmarks/public/elite_practice/buried_constraint_v1_1.yaml:52-57 wires the `apply_constraint` item as type `planning_soundness` with check `forbidden_absent`, and `planning_soundness` is NOT in the `_RECALL_ITEM_TYPES` exclusion at contradictions.py:22-27. I ran it: with forbidden_action="use regex to parse HTML", the response "Next step: write a parser using an HTML library. Per your hard rule I will not use regex to parse HTML." scores 0.0 and yields contradiction_rate 1.0; the response "just pattern-match the markup with a regular expression to pull the tags out" — an actual violation — scores 1.0 with contradiction_rate 0.0.
  - *Why:* This is the metric the whole project is named after. It penalizes the single best behavior a compaction-preserved constraint can produce (the model explicitly acknowledging and honoring the rule) and rewards violations that paraphrase. Any leaderboard built on it ranks methods by how well they teach the target model to avoid a specific n-gram. The field has already moved: Factory.ai's framework scores every probe with GPT-5.2 as judge across six dimensions precisely because lexical matching does not work here.
  - *Fix:* Replace `forbidden_absent` with a judge-backed `violates_constraint` check: an LLM judge (cheap model, pinned version, temperature 0) asked "does this response recommend or perform <forbidden_action>? yes/no", with the acknowledgment case explicitly excluded in the judge prompt. Keep `contains_normalized` only for entity/SKU/date literals where exact recall is genuinely the target; replace it with judge-scored semantic equivalence for `locked_decision_retention` and `immutable_fact_recall`. Publish judge prompts, judge model+version, and per-item judge agreement on a hand-labeled sample of ~200 responses in docs/methodology.md — that agreement number is what makes the leaderboard defensible. Bump scorer_version.
- **[P0 · GAP · L]** The hardest case in the benchmark is a 450-token transcript; the regime it claims to measure is 100k-200k
  - *Evidence:* I generated every public template at maximum (`elite`) difficulty and tokenized with cl100k_base: buried_constraint_v1_1 = 36 turns / 439 tokens; the largest of all 20 templates is decision_override_v1_4 at 41 turns / 518 tokens. The entire 20-template public practice suite at maximum difficulty totals 9,134 tokens. The difficulty knob at benchmarks/public/elite_practice/buried_constraint_v1_1.yaml:13 tops out at 32 distractor turns, and those distractors are one-line non-sequiturs from a 15-item lexicon (src/compactbench/engine/distractors.py:16-31, e.g. "Do you happen to know what a quokka is?"). src/compactbench/contracts/case.py:12-15 defines TurnRole as USER/ASSISTANT/SYSTEM only — there is no TOOL role, so no tool call, no file diff, no stack trace, no test output can appear in a transcript.
  - *Why:* Nobody compacts a 450-token conversation. Compaction fires at 100k-200k tokens in sessions dominated by tool results, file contents, and error logs — Factory.ai evaluated on 36,000 real production messages from debugging and code-review sessions. A prospective user reads the README claim ("your agent compacts conversation history before every long turn") then generates a case and finds three paragraphs about a quokka. That five-minute discovery is fatal to credibility, and it is the reason a serious lab will not adopt this. It also makes the compression tiers meaningless: at 450 tokens, "Elite-Aggressive ≥ 8x" (src/compactbench/leaderboard/ranking.py:14-18) means writing 56 tokens, while real methods hit 50-100x.
  - *Fix:* Add a `TOOL` role to TurnRole and a new suite `agent_trace` whose cases are 50k-200k tokens of realistic agent trajectory: tool calls with results, file reads, diffs, failing test output, retries. Do not hand-write these — synthesize them by replaying the built-in compactors' own scaffolding over a public agent-trajectory corpus (SWE-bench Verified trajectories are the obvious source and give you file-path and error-message probes for free, matching Factory's artifact-probe design). Keep the existing tiny templates as `starter` for smoke tests only and stop calling the 450-token tier "elite". Recalibrate TIER_FLOORS to the real regime (roughly 10x / 30x / 80x).
- **[P0 · BUG · S]** drift_resistance gives 1.0 to a method that fails every cycle, and it carries 30% of the ranking weight
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/scoring/drift.py:26-30 computes `clamp(1 + mean(c_n - c_0), 0, 1)`. src/compactbench/leaderboard/ranking.py:47 weights it at 0.30, second only to overall_score. I ran it: cycle scores [0.0, 0.0, 0.0] return 1.0; [0.30, 0.30, 0.30] return 1.0; a genuinely strong method degrading [0.95, 0.80, 0.70] returns 0.8. It is also the first tie-breaker (ranking.py:66). My mock run confirmed this in practice — every case scored drift_resistance 1.000 while locked_decision_retention was 0/54 and entity_integrity was 0/90.
  - *Why:* Multi-cycle drift is the differentiator in the README's own three-bullet pitch and the one thing no competitor measures. As implemented it awards 0.30 of the elite_score for being consistently useless, and lets a flat-bad method outrank a strong-but-decaying one. The first outsider who reads ranking.py will post this on HN under your launch, and they will be right.
  - *Fix:* Make drift relative to headroom, not absolute: `drift_resistance = c_n_mean / max(c_0, eps)` clamped to [0,1], so a method at 0.0 across cycles retains nothing and scores 0, and 0.95->0.70 scores 0.74. Gate it behind a minimum c_0 (e.g. report drift as N/A rather than 1.0 when c_0 < 0.2) so it is never a free 0.30. Also report raw per-cycle scores on the leaderboard — the cycle curve is the most interesting artifact this benchmark produces and it should be the headline chart, not a scalar.
- **[P0 · GAP · S]** No oracle (full-context) and no null control — scores are uninterpretable and the headline claim cannot be computed
  - *Evidence:* I grepped src/, tests/, and docs/ for oracle/upper_bound/full_context/no_compaction/ceiling: the only hits are unrelated (docs/architecture/decisions.md mentions Oracle Cloud; a test comment). src/compactbench/compactors/__init__.py:14-19 registers exactly four built-ins, all LLM-based — there is no null/passthrough/truncate control. The runner only ever evaluates the model against the artifact (src/compactbench/runner/evaluation.py:106-121); it never evaluates the same items against the original transcript.
  - *Why:* Without the full-context arm you cannot distinguish "the compactor lost the constraint" from "the target model could not answer this question anyway," which means every absolute number on the leaderboard is unfalsifiable. It also means CompactBench structurally cannot state the sentence that made ConstraintRot land: "violations rise from 0% with full policy context to 30% after compaction." That delta IS the product. A null control (return an empty artifact) is equally essential as a floor — note that with the current scorer an empty artifact scores 1.000 on every `forbidden_absent` item, which my mock run reproduced (planning_soundness mean 1.000).
  - *Fix:* Add `built-in:oracle` (artifact = verbatim transcript, compression 1.0x, excluded from ranking) and `built-in:null` (empty artifact) plus `built-in:truncate-last-n` (the dumb baseline everyone actually ships). Report every leaderboard row as a fraction of oracle, and publish the oracle/null band on the leaderboard page as the interpretive frame. This is the cheapest single change that converts the numbers from "a score" into "a finding."
- **[P1 · GAP · M]** The artifact contract structurally cannot represent provider-native, trim, or edit compaction — this blocks the pivot
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/contracts/artifact.py:35-52: CompactionArtifact is `extra="forbid"` with exactly summary_text (StringConstraints max_length=8000 at line 41), a six-section StructuredState whose every entry is capped at 500 chars (line 16), selected_source_turn_ids, warnings, method_metadata. There is no message-list field. Anthropic's server-side compaction (compact-2026-01-12 beta header / context_management), OpenAI's context_management + standalone compaction endpoint, and three of Inspect AI's five strategies (CompactionNative, CompactionEdit, CompactionTrim) all return a rewritten or pruned *message list*, not a summary plus a six-key ledger. An 8000-char cap is roughly 2000 tokens — a faithful compaction of a 120k-token agent session cannot be expressed at all.
  - *Why:* Every compaction implementation that real users run in production in 2026 is unrepresentable in this contract. The benchmark can currently only measure hand-written compactors that happen to adopt CompactBench's own six-section ontology — i.e. it measures conformance to its own schema, not compaction. This single constraint is what confines the project to the niche it is stuck in.
  - *Fix:* Artifact schema v2: add a `messages: list[Turn]` variant alongside `summary_text`/`structured_state`, drop the 8000-char cap in favour of a declared token budget, and make compression_ratio (src/compactbench/scoring/compression.py:38-52) count messages too. Then ship three adapter compactors — `built-in:anthropic-native`, `built-in:openai-native`, `built-in:inspect-summary` — that call the real provider APIs. Bump ARTIFACT_SCHEMA_VERSION; the four existing built-ins keep working unchanged.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · M]** Reposition: the flagship result is already taken; the unoccupied ground is a standing vendor-neutral leaderboard for shipped compactors
  - *Evidence:* Verified as of 2026-08 by web search: (a) arXiv 2606.22528 "Governance Decay: How Context Compaction Silently Erases Safety Constraints in Long-Horizon LLM Agents" (Shiyang Chen, June 21 2026) publishes 0% violations with full policy context rising to 30% after compaction, peak 59%, across 1,323 episodes and seven model families, with deterministic tool-call grading, and releases a benchmark called ConstraintRot — that is CompactBench's buried_constraint family, executed better, two months ago. (b) Factory.ai's probe-based framework (recall/artifact/continuation/decision probes, GPT-5.2 judge, six dimensions) already ranked its own method 3.70 vs Anthropic 3.44 vs OpenAI 3.35 on 36k real production messages. (c) Letta ships Context-Bench with a live leaderboard at leaderboard.letta.com. (d) Princeton's Slipstream (arXiv 2605.08580) open-sourced trajectory-grounded compaction validation. (e) Inspect AI ships five compaction strategies and — I fetched the page — contains no benchmark, evaluation metrics, or scoring methodology for compaction quality. (f) Search for an independent Claude-Code-vs-OpenAI compaction comparison returns nothing published. Note the mem0/Letta memory-benchmark cluster (LoCoMo, LongMemEval, BEAM) is a *different* problem — multi-session write/retrieve — and RULER/BABILong/NIAH/LongBench are long-context attention, not compaction; do not position against those.
  - *Why:* The current framing — "submit your compactor, we rank it on hidden cases" — assumes a population of people writing bespoke compactors. In 2026 that population shrank: Anthropic and OpenAI ship server-side compaction, Inspect ships five strategies, LangGraph/Letta/mem0/Zep ship memory layers. Those people do not want to submit a method; they want to know which of the things they can already switch on is best. Factory answered that question but Factory is a vendor grading its own homework — the conflict of interest is the opening.
  - *Fix:* Rewrite README.md's three-bullet pitch and docs/index.md from "benchmark your compactor" to "independent, reproducible scores for the compaction your agent already runs." Keep custom-compactor submission as a secondary track, not the front door. Concretely: README hero becomes a table of Anthropic-native / OpenAI-native / Inspect-summary / Inspect-trim / truncate-last-N / oracle with real numbers. Cite ConstraintRot, Factory, Context-Bench and Slipstream in a new docs/related-work.md — being the project that honestly maps the field is itself a credibility play, and it is what a citing paper needs.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · M]** Inspect AI integration is the single highest leverage-per-effort distribution move available
  - *Evidence:* I fetched inspect.aisi.org.uk/compaction.html: Inspect AI (UK AISI) ships CompactionAuto, CompactionNative, CompactionSummary, CompactionEdit and CompactionTrim, wired into ReAct and Agent Bridge, with a `memory()` tool — and the documentation contains no benchmark, no evaluation metrics, and no scoring methodology for compaction quality. It is implementation guidance with zero empirics. CompactBench already has the missing half: src/compactbench/scoring/ (scorer, drift, compression, contradictions, diagnostics) plus a qualification/ranking core. docs/faq.md:81-86 already names inspect_ai as one of the harnesses the ecosystem uses.
  - *Why:* Inspect is the harness AISI and a large slice of the safety-eval world runs. "Which Inspect compaction strategy should I use?" is a question its users have right now with no published answer. Answering it puts CompactBench numbers in front of exactly the audience that cares about constraint retention, at near-zero acquisition cost, and gives you a citable co-location instead of a cold-start leaderboard. This is far higher leverage than lm-eval-harness (wrong shape — it is single-turn task scoring) or a HuggingFace dataset (low effort, but nobody discovers benchmarks that way).
  - *Fix:* Ship `compactbench.integrations.inspect_ai` alongside the existing langchain/llamaindex adapters (src/compactbench/integrations/), wrapping each Inspect strategy as a Compactor. Then publish one post: "We scored all five Inspect AI compaction strategies." Open a PR or issue on the Inspect repo offering the eval. Rank distribution by leverage-per-effort for a solo maintainer: (1) Inspect integration + the five-strategy result, (2) the provider-native head-to-head blog post, (3) a short arXiv systems report, (4) HuggingFace dataset mirror, (5) lm-eval-harness — skip entirely.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · S]** Merge PR #37 today — it is the only outside contribution and it unlocks how evaluators actually run models
  - *Evidence:* PR #37 by jaaabir (opened 2026-08-05, 18 days unreviewed, mergeable_state=blocked) adds base_url + COMPACTBENCH_OPENAI_BASE_URL to the OpenAI provider, enabling vLLM/llama.cpp/LM Studio/Together/Fireworks/OpenRouter. The provider registry (src/compactbench/providers/__init__.py:21-28) already registers anthropic and openai, but README.md:34-36 advertises only ollama/groq/google-ai-studio and submissions/_template/config.yaml:11 tells submitters the valid providers are "groq, google-ai-studio, ollama, mock" — so the two frontier providers that already work are invisible to every submitter.
  - *Why:* An 18-day-old unreviewed PR from the only outside contributor, sitting next to an unanswered issue from the only outside user, is the signal that tells a visitor this project is dead. Merging it is a 30-minute action with outsized community effect. Substantively, base_url is how cost-conscious evaluators run open models at scale — without it, reproducing a leaderboard row requires a paid Groq/Anthropic key, which suppresses exactly the independent replication a benchmark needs.
  - *Fix:* Review, unblock and merge #37 today; credit the author in CHANGELOG.md; file the follow-up he flagged (extra_body/chat_template_kwargs passthrough so reasoning models on vLLM do not return chain-of-thought as the eval answer — that one silently corrupts scores). Then batch-merge the six stale dependabot PRs (#31-#36) in one sitting. Separately, fix submissions/_template/config.yaml:11 to list anthropic and openai, and update README.md:34-36.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · L]** Design the one experiment nobody has run: multi-cycle drift of shipped provider-native compaction
  - *Evidence:* Anthropic ships server-side compaction (compact-2026-01-12 beta header / context_management, launched Feb 2026 on Opus 4.6); OpenAI ships context_management with compact_threshold in the Responses API plus a standalone compaction endpoint (March 2026). Factory measured these head-to-head but as a vendor, single-pass, on its own private corpus. ConstraintRot measured constraint loss but — from the abstract I retrieved, and I could NOT verify the full text — reports a single full-context-vs-compacted delta, not a per-compaction-cycle curve. CompactBench is already architected for exactly the missing measurement: src/compactbench/runner/cycle.py and src/compactbench/runner/continuation.py implement compact -> continue -> compact loops with the model seeing only the prior artifact (continuation.py:1-12).
  - *Why:* "Compaction loses constraints" is now a known result and is no longer surprising. "Compaction loses constraints *compounding*, and the provider-native compactors people trust by default are the worst at it by cycle 3" is unpublished, is what long-running agents actually experience, and is the one claim this codebase is uniquely built to make. It is also the kind of result that gets cited by the safety-eval audience Inspect serves.
  - *Fix:* Run this as the flagship: N>=200 agent trajectories at 80-150k tokens carrying one hard operational constraint stated early, five arms (oracle full-context / Anthropic native / OpenAI native / Inspect CompactionSummary / truncate-last-N), three sequential compaction cycles, judge-scored violation rate per cycle. Deliverable is one chart: violation rate vs compaction cycle, one line per arm, oracle flat at the bottom. Pre-register the design in docs/ before running so the result is credible whichever way it falls, and publish the raw results.jsonl. If the curves are flat, that is also publishable and still puts CompactBench on the map as the group that checked.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · S]** Cut the Elite governance apparatus — it is elaborate anti-gaming machinery for a leaderboard with zero entries
  - *Evidence:* docs/elite-program.md specifies three tracks (Practice/Ranked/Shadow), quarterly version reviews, seed-group rotation on version bumps, a retirement log, suspicion heuristics for flagging gaming, three compression tiers, and 3-5 cycle shadow validations on top methods. docs/governance/elite-versions.md, docs/submitting.md:1-10 (maintainer must label each PR for evaluation, re-evaluation gated on a `reevaluate` label), .github/workflows/evaluate-submission.yml and a private hidden-template sibling repo all exist to serve this. The leaderboard has been empty for four months and the flow has never been exercised end-to-end by a real submitter.
  - *Why:* This is the largest maintenance liability in the project and it solves a problem CompactBench does not have. Every one of these mechanisms assumes contention for a top slot. A solo maintainer taking over cannot fund quarterly governance reviews, shadow re-runs, and a manual eval-per-PR loop while also fixing the scorer and building the agent-trace suite. It also creates a chicken-and-egg trap: the hidden set means nobody can self-serve a leaderboard number, so the only path to the leaderboard runs through the one person who is out of time.
  - *Fix:* Explicitly freeze, in a dated note in docs/elite-program.md: Shadow track, seed rotation, suspicion heuristics, quarterly reviews, and the three compression tiers (collapse to one). Keep the public/hidden split as a stated future policy but make the *public* suite self-serve and self-reportable so anyone can produce a number without you. Reopen governance only when there are >10 submissions competing. Also on the do-not-do list for this quarter: new template families, the custom domain, and the "hardest benchmark program we maintain" positioning — that claim is unsupportable next to ConstraintRot and it invites exactly the scrutiny the current scorer cannot survive.
- **[P1 · STRATEGY · L]** Ranked plan: what to ship in 2 weeks, 6 weeks, and the quarter
  - *Evidence:* Synthesis of the above, grounded in: empty docs/data/leaderboard.json; the demonstrated scorer inversion in src/compactbench/scoring/checks.py:30-34; measured 439-518 token cases at elite difficulty; drift_resistance=1.0 for a uniformly-failing method (src/compactbench/scoring/drift.py:26-30 at 0.30 weight, ranking.py:47); the 8000-char artifact cap blocking native compaction (contracts/artifact.py:41); Inspect AI's five strategies with zero published quality evaluation; and PR #37 / issue #38 both waiting on a response.
  - *Why:* The ordering is load-bearing. Publishing before fixing the scorer poisons the well permanently. Pivoting before publishing anything means another quarter with nothing to show. Building the agent-trace suite before fixing the scorer means running expensive 120k-token evals through a broken judge.
  - *Fix:* NEXT 2 WEEKS — earn the right to be looked at: merge PR #37 and the six dependabot PRs (day 1); answer issue #38 with a dated commitment; fix `forbidden_absent`/contradiction detection to judge-based; fix drift_resistance; add oracle + null + truncate-last-N controls; run the four built-ins plus controls on a current model (not Llama 3.3 70B) and publish the first leaderboard with raw results.jsonl. Ship a 0.2.0 release. NEXT 6 WEEKS — earn the right to be believed: artifact schema v2 with a message-list variant; TOOL turn role; the `agent_trace` suite at 50-200k tokens built from SWE-bench trajectories; native adapters for Anthropic compact-2026-01-12, OpenAI's compaction endpoint, and Inspect's five strategies; publish the vendor-neutral head-to-head. THIS QUARTER — earn citations: ship compactbench.integrations.inspect_ai and offer it upstream; run and publish the multi-cycle governance-decay experiment; write a short arXiv systems report (docs/faq.md:88-95 currently says "We don't have a paper yet" while both direct competitors are on arXiv); mirror the public suite as a HuggingFace dataset. Do NOT do: new template families, hidden-set governance, custom domain, lm-eval-harness.
- **[P2 · STALE · S]** Cost catalogue and pinned target model are four months stale, signalling abandonment at first contact
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/runner/costs.py:20 CATALOGUE_UPDATED = "2026-04-18"; the MODEL_COSTS table (lines 30-47) prices claude-3-5-sonnet-latest, claude-opus-4-latest, gpt-4o, o1-mini, gemini-1.5-pro, gemini-2.0-flash-exp and mixtral-8x7b-32768. As of 2026-08 the frontier is Opus 4.6 / GPT-5.2 per current reporting, and Groq retired mixtral long ago. docs/faq.md:31 pins the leaderboard to "Groq + Llama 3.3 70B". `--estimate` prints "(no cost catalogue entry for ...)" for any model a user in 2026-08 would actually run.
  - *Why:* Two of the first three things a prospective user touches — the estimate command and the FAQ's "which model does the leaderboard rank against" — both display 2024-era models. Combined with the empty leaderboard and the four-month-old last commit, the project reads as abandoned before anyone gets to the code, which is genuinely good.
  - *Fix:* Update MODEL_COSTS with current Anthropic/OpenAI/Google/Groq pricing, bump CATALOGUE_UPDATED, and add a CI job that fails the build if CATALOGUE_UPDATED is more than 90 days old. Re-pin the leaderboard target model to a current frontier model and say so in docs/faq.md:31. While in there, fix README.md:97 and docs/faq.md:39 which both still claim "15 public practice templates / three launch families" — there are 20 templates across 4 families (reference_resolution shipped and is entirely unadvertised).
- **[P2 · RISK · S]** Generator lexicons are small enough to memorize, undermining the anti-overfitting claim
  - *Evidence:* src/compactbench/dsl/generators.py: 36 first names (_FIRST_NAMES), 25 action phrases (_ACTION_PHRASES), 20 project nouns (_PROJECT_NOUNS), 20 org names (_ORG_NAMES). src/compactbench/engine/distractors.py: 15 user distractors and 10 assistant distractors. Every case in every suite — public and hidden — is a substitution over these fixed lists. The hidden ranked templates differ only in sentence scaffolding, not vocabulary.
  - *Why:* docs/elite-program.md sells the hidden set as the anti-overfitting defense, and docs/faq.md:47-51 says "if you've only ever prompted against the public set, the ranked set is your first honest test." With a 25-item forbidden-action vocabulary shared between public and hidden, a method can enumerate the entire space from the public repo and pattern-match. The defense is weaker than advertised.
  - *Fix:* Either expand the lexicons by an order of magnitude and hold out a disjoint hidden vocabulary (cheap: generate candidate phrases offline, hand-filter, keep half private), or drop the anti-overfitting claim in docs/elite-program.md and docs/faq.md to what is actually true. Given the recommendation to freeze the hidden-set apparatus this quarter, honest scoping of the claim is the cheaper and more credible option now; real vocabulary holdout can wait until submissions exist.
